The following craft sections (2–6, 8–9) are included verbatim. Sections 1 and 7's legal discussion is not drafting guidance. Use original names, world, systems, events and phrasing; the owner names the cast.

## 2. Sentence rhythm

| Metric | Target |
|---|---|
| Mean sentence length | 14.6 words |
| Median sentence length | 11 words |
| Spread (population stdev) | ~26 words — wide, meaning short declaratives sit right next to long compound-complex sentences, not clustered near the mean |
| Share of very short sentences (≤5 words) | 27.7% |
| Share of very long sentences (≥40 words) | 3.3% |

**Read:** roughly 1 sentence in 4 is a hard, short beat (often a single clause landing a
punch after a longer setup) — this is the "short sentence for impact" technique, not
uniform mid-length prose. Target this ratio, not just the mean; a manuscript that hits mean
14.6 but has no short-sentence spikes will not read the same.

## 3. Paragraph and scene rhythm (ASR-inferred proxy)

Whisper transcripts carry no typographic paragraph marks — this section is inferred from
pause gaps between narrated speech segments, not a count of the print manuscript's actual
paragraph breaks. Treat as directional, not exact.

| Metric | Target |
|---|---|
| Inferred paragraph length (pause ≥0.6s) | median 18 words, mean 26.8 words — short, frequent beats |
| Inferred scene/section length (pause ≥1.6s) | mean ~950 words between scene-level breaks, ~8.7 scene breaks per 10k words |

**Read:** short paragraphs are the default texture, with scenes running roughly 900–1000
words before a beat change (POV settle, location change, or time-skip). Avoid long
unbroken expository blocks; break early and often.

## 4. Progression-mechanic ("change frequency") callout rate

| Metric | Target |
|---|---|
| Progression/stat-vocabulary density | ~58 hits per 10k words (rank, growth-analog, spec, level-up, stat-block language) |
| Placement | front-loaded — the opening third of the book carries roughly 1.7x the progression-callout density of the middle and closing thirds (900 vs. 471 vs. 514 hits, by third) |

**Read:** the system gets *established* early and hard, then recedes into the background
as character and plot take over — it does not stay evenly dense through the whole book.
Build your own progression system's vocabulary (do not reuse his axis names — see §7), but
match this front-loaded shape: teach the mechanic aggressively in the first third, then let
it surface only at genuine turning points afterward.

## 5. Character-development beat cadence

Methodology: named-character mention within ~120 characters of a reflection/interiority
marker ("realized," "decided," "for the first time," "swore," "no longer," etc.) — a
heuristic count, not a scene-by-scene read.

| Metric | Target |
|---|---|
| Protagonist development-moment rate | 4.5 per 10k words |
| Protagonist distribution across thirds | roughly even (41 / 56 / 50 — a slight middle-third bump, not backloaded) |
| Supporting-cast development-moment rate | an order of magnitude lower per character (0.1–0.7 per 10k words each), spread across 8–10 named characters rather than concentrated in one deuteragonist |

**Read:** growth beats for the lead land steadily throughout, not saved up for a finale —
and the supporting cast gets *many* small beats each rather than one character getting a
big secondary arc. If your story gives one best-friend character 80% of the secondary
development budget, that's a different shape than this book's.

## 6. POV structure

| Metric | Target |
|---|---|
| Protagonist POV share | ~87% of chapter-word-volume (combining his given-name and surname references) |
| Secondary-character POV/focus share | ~13%, spread across 4–5 named characters at 2.5–3.7% each — brief cutaways, not sustained alternating-POV chapters |

**Read:** this is single-POV-dominant with occasional short cutaway scenes to a handful of
other named characters, not a true multi-POV structure. If you want to signal "feels like
Iron Prince," don't build alternating-chapter dual POV — keep the lead's POV overwhelming
and let other characters get glimpses, not chapters.

## 8. Supporting craft signals (secondary, use to taste)

- **Filler-word discipline:** "that" runs hot (92.1 per 10k words) — a known, common genre
  tic, not a target to imitate; if anything, tighten below this. Other fillers ("just,"
  "almost," "felt," "seemed") sit in the 5–25-per-10k band — moderate, not aggressively
  self-edited, not sloppy either.
- **Adverb density:** ~161 -ly adverbs per 10k words — fairly high; this is a book that
  lets adverbs carry tone rather than insisting on pure show-don't-tell. Not a craft flaw
  to correct, a genre-register signal to optionally match.
- **Dialogue-tag (reporting-verb) density:** ~41 per 10k words — moderate dialogue presence,
  consistent with an action/interiority-forward book rather than a dialogue-driven one.
- **Combat-vocabulary density:** ~22 per 10k words (strike/block/dodge/blade/impact/etc.) —
  present throughout, not just in set-piece fight chapters.
- **Readability:** Flesch Reading Ease 72.3, Flesch-Kincaid grade 6.8 — accessible YA/adult
  crossover register, short-to-medium sentence construction, low jargon-density outside the
  progression-vocabulary spikes.
- **Repetition discipline:** common connective 3-grams ("out of the," "one of the," "for a
  moment") sit at 2–4 per 10k words — normal English-prose background rate, not a
  distinctive tic to replicate. Don't chase these; they're noise, not signature.

---

## 9. One-paragraph brief for the drafting agent

> Write single-POV-dominant (~85–90% lead-character screen time), short-paragraph,
> pause-heavy prose (median paragraph ~18 words, scenes breaking roughly every 900–1000
> words). Mix short punch sentences (~28% of all sentences ≤5 words) against longer
> compound-complex ones (mean 14.6 words, wide spread) rather than settling into uniform
> mid-length sentences. Establish your progression system hard and often in the first
> third of the book, then let it recede to occasional high-stakes surfacing afterward. Land
> a protagonist growth/realization beat roughly every 2,200 words, spread evenly across the
> book rather than saved for the climax, and give 8–10 supporting characters small
> development beats each rather than concentrating growth in one deuteragonist. Moderate
> dialogue-tag and adverb use; accessible ~7th-grade reading level; combat vocabulary
> present in the background throughout, not confined to fight scenes. Every name, every
> system-label, every scene: yours, invented, original.


---

## NUMERICAL REVIEW

# Numerical formula alignment — after the movement

This is an informed editorial pass, not a blind cold read. Read the supplied
formula and full movement, then report target, observed result, method, coverage
and uncertainty. Keep numerical alignment separate from reader scores.

- Sentence mean, median, population standard deviation; shares ≤5 and ≥40 words.
- Written paragraph mean/median and actual scene/section boundaries. Compare with
  the requested targets, but do not equate typography with ASR pause segmentation.
- Flesch Reading Ease and Flesch-Kincaid grade. Identify the sentence tokenizer and
  syllable estimator; proper names/system terms can affect estimates.
- Progression vocabulary hits per 10,000 words, and placement by equal word-count
  book thirds once available. Define a project-specific lexicon before counting;
  do not borrow the source novel's names. An incomplete book cannot confirm the
  full front-loading ratio. Report provisional cumulative coverage instead.
- Lead development moments and supporting-cast distribution: cite actual choices
  or realizations and distinguish manually identified beats from regex proxies.
- POV word share: identify viewpoint segments, not the most frequently mentioned
  name. Track the lead and secondary viewpoints against the book-level allocation.
- Secondary signals: -ly words, reporting verbs, combat terms, fillers and repeated
  phrases. Publish lexicons and counting definitions. A suffix count is not proof
  that every matched word is an adverb. Preserve optional/non-target distinctions.

Use reproducible counts when tooling exists. Otherwise mark a metric UNMEASURED;
never estimate a precise score by intuition. Save the method or command with the
report so later runs use the same definitions. Inspect ASR reference limitations
without discarding the owner's chosen drafting targets.

Locate meaningful departures, especially uniform sentence lengths, missing short
beats, reading level drift, dense paragraphs, late teaching or a coequal second
lead replacing the requested POV shape. Consolidate the most important repairs
with continuity findings into the existing bounded author repair. Protect exact
owner text and successful scenes. Do not iterate indefinitely to hit decimals or
hide irreconcilable targets. Bring consequential formula conflicts to the owner.

---

## PROJECT EVIDENCE — books/book-2680-opening/packets/MOVEMENT-004.md

# Movement 004 — What the Record Lost

- PWA book id: `kindling-2680-book-01`
- Series id: `kindling-2680`
- Author: Claude Opus 5.5
- Seat: Monroe Jackson 1.3.0 / O'Connor 1.3.0
- Chapters: 25–32, one continuous creative run
- Working movement length: approximately 45,000–55,000 words, governed by complete
  scenes rather than padding or compression
- Owner names remain locked. Check `universe/NAME_REGISTRY.md` before inventing a
  necessary minor name. Do not import names from the research corpus.

## Where we enter

Movement Three closes on Sunday the third of the new month. Kiva has completed one
correct deliberate two-door sequence, Stride into stance, under Hollis Vane's field
direction and outside her recovery-only certificate. It lasted about nine seconds,
spent 0.31 dram at a Draw near twelve, left her nose bleeding and arm slack, and has
not been repeated. Orla will sign nothing until she witnesses six sanctioned successes.
Team Seven funded those six tries under three written conditions: a sanctioned mast,
Orla on the crate, and Noa reading only after. Kiva is stood down until the thirteenth:
no lanes, floors, landings, or lit doors. Her knee remains strapped; her first two left
fingers return only to their previous slow, late state. Permanent loss remains reserved
for Movement Five.

The Pit response made the anomaly publicly searchable without explaining it. Team
Seven is ninth of thirty-one by yield and attached on the second step to Graywater.
Its reconciled book holds 2.0 drams sealed medical, 1.0 returned to Wren House at par,
3.0 spent on a purse sheet and poles, 2.0 reserved for the six sequence tries, and 1.6
held at 0.4 a head. The company recovered 419 of 480 drams; Team Seven's bag was 51.2.
The Pit's new rule requires a lit gate. The Pit remains shut until about the thirteenth.

Anwen and Kiva have not repaired the dark-floor breach between them. Anwen begins her
six-week Graywater summer line on the tenth. Kellan is weighing a Halden team. Perrin
has a Stonehand second-team autumn place. Noa knows the keeper recorded the Pit as T-0
and does not know what that means. Kiva privately holds four T-0 entries—2446, 2604,
2676, and the Pit—and an unread margin from 2446. She has told none of the team and
intends to ask the Compact Library's reader herself. Orla knows there is a bearer's book
at the Home Line hall and has asked Vane for it. Vane found an earlier hand in the
field-direction ledger but cannot read or date it.

Read repaired Chapter Twenty-Four and `provenance/MOVEMENT-003-CONTINUITY.md` in full.
Carry forward every date, injury, certification, ash entry, relationship, recruitment
choice, and knowledge boundary. The repair recheck governs if it records a required
mechanical correction before drafting begins.

## What this movement is for

Let recovery time create story rather than a skipped wait. Public results, the seventh
reed, the disk's one warmth, the T-0 markings, and the rich dirty spring cause different
people to search different records for different reasons. Kiva pursues the Compact
Library's reader and the 2446 margin; Noa follows instrument and sensing-mast evidence
within his consent boundary; Orla and Vane pursue the bearer and field-direction books.
Founder histories, ash patents, professional logs, and old fever accounts should
disagree in useful, human ways: copied omissions, proprietary claims, renamed methods,
or missing reasons. Do not turn this into eight chapters of adults delivering archives.
Evidence must change choices, relationships, access, or risk on the page.

Pay the dark-floor aftermath between Kiva and Anwen through actions and imperfect
conversation. Anwen's summer line is her own earned future. Kellan, Perrin, Noa, Jude,
Pru, Lowen, Selka, Gower, and Rhea may make independent placement or recruitment
decisions, but do not expand the viewpoint roster simply to report them. Preserve the
single-lead-dominant book shape and use only purposeful brief cutaways.

After the thirteenth, let the six sanctioned sequence attempts begin or meaningfully
advance. Kiva does not leap from one success to six. A familiar step should compress;
the new complication should expand. The teaching should clarify sequence, ash cost,
body position, abort conditions, and why a correct transition is not a simultaneous
stack. At least one attempt should fail for a reason different from the first fourteen.
At least one supporting person should have to correct Kiva rather than admire her.

The movement's physical spine is a supervised professional shadow shift that becomes
a real multi-type Riftspawn call. Use a district whose people and infrastructure matter,
not a spectacle floor. Introduce several comprehensible spawn expressions whose
interaction creates the danger; do not merely enlarge the Pit string. Collegiate habits
should initially fail because students optimize for score, fixed lanes, or a single
opponent. They recover only when roles, reasons, handoffs, aborts, rescue, closure,
kill, and clean recovery are declared again. Professionals retain the work beyond the
students' license. Give the response developed tactical space, clear terrain, starting
positions, changing decisions, injury/fatigue, and a recovery/reconciliation aftermath.
Clean ash matters because the threatened district needs it and contaminated capture
would compound the emergency.

The professional shift must teach the difference between being impressive and being
useful without humiliating the students into irrelevance. Kiva may contribute within
her actual paper, observation, carrying, recovery, or one bounded trained action, but
the disk and fever fragment supply no tactic. The team should leave with a concrete
correction they can attempt again and with a sharper understanding of professional
rift-company standings: lives, closure, infrastructure, usable ash, and violations.

By the movement's close, Kiva learns from accountable evidence that the disk was
released or assigned to a bearer; she does not learn its cosmic origin or receive a
chosen-one guarantee. A pre-Kiva account contains an unmistakable fragment of the
Unroofed Sky. The fragment confirms shared imagery across time but not cause,
interpretation, timing, or solution. Let the discovery cost trust, access, certainty,
or safety. It should open Movement Five's fight over ownership, proof, sponsorship,
and the Regional Measure—not replace that conflict with an answer.

## What must remain true / withheld

- One source, three traditions. Soul supplies owned intention and pattern; finite ash
  supplies ownerless fuel. Ash cannot purchase judgment or automatic experience.
- Draw is spent ash divided by the governing expectation; lower is more efficient.
  Rank, Hold, Draw, Call, control, yield, grade, and resonance remain separate.
- Kiva has one successful deliberate two-door sequence and fourteen earlier misses.
  She may progress through the six funded attempts, but cannot casually master the
  sequence, stabilize all three doors, reach Glory, or gain a secret top rank.
- The disk warmed once only after the correct sequence. It does not teach, speak, map,
  answer, trigger on demand, or solve the professional response.
- The Unroofed Sky is involuntary evidence, not tactical prophecy. Do not identify the
  cause of the Fall, prove the filter theory, explain the disk's true origin, name the
  Makers, or state whether the Glory figures cause or answer filter loss.
- Kiva remains stood down through the twelfth. The six sanctioned attempts and any
  professional field participation must respect dates, medical limits, certification,
  and written supervision. Do not erase the knee or finger state.
- Anwen leaves on the tenth for six weeks. Do not keep her conveniently on Team Seven
  afterward. Her relationship with Kiva may remain unresolved while she goes.
- Team Seven's ledger stays exact. The sealed W-trace medical reserve is not training
  fuel. Every new spend, yield, share, return, and investment is reconciled.
- Kiva has kept the T-0 papers from the team. Noa knows only what the keeper and his own
  measurements gave him. Orla and Vane have their separate evidence. Nobody silently
  acquires another person's knowledge.
- The 10:24:40 pattern remains unexplained. The Flask string is the last new mechanics-
  heavy spawn expression introduced at that weight; this movement's multi-type call
  should deepen established rules through interaction rather than open a new textbook.
- Lowen remains an adaptive legitimate rival. Selka remains unresolved institutional
  pressure. Neither becomes a secret villain.
- Mara's ninth-month re-sit, Anwen's line, Perrin's autumn place, Kellan's Halden choice,
  and the Crooked Line's remaining meets continue whether or not Kiva watches them.
- Preserve accessible one-pass prose, narration-ready punctuation, short-paragraph
  rhythm, varied sentence weight, warmth and humor, and full tactical scenes. Do not
  inflate dialogue to chase a statistical mean or reduce a successful fight to summary.

## What the author owns

Own the eight-chapter route, archive locations, contradictory documents, interpersonal
beats, exact six-try progress, shadow-company structure, district, terrain, multi-type
spawn interaction, professional tactics, rescue, kill and recovery, humor, purposeful
cutaways, and the precise form of the pre-Kiva Unroofed Sky fragment. The author may
let the map change when the prose earns a stronger route, but must record surviving
changes in the continuity checkpoint rather than silently altering canon.

Use movement-sized causality. The archive evidence should affect field judgment; the
placement choices should change who is available; the professional response should
make the record dispute material; the recovered evidence should create Movement Five's
institutional pressure.

## Output and stop condition

Write complete chapters to:

- `manuscript/chapter-25.md`
- `manuscript/chapter-26.md`
- `manuscript/chapter-27.md`
- `manuscript/chapter-28.md`
- `manuscript/chapter-29.md`
- `manuscript/chapter-30.md`
- `manuscript/chapter-31.md`
- `manuscript/chapter-32.md`

Then write `provenance/MOVEMENT-004-CONTINUITY.md`, append the actual model, session,
prompt, word counts, and edition status to `AUTHORSHIP.md`, and update `STATE_LEDGER.md`
only from surviving prose. Stop after the complete first draft and records. Do not
review, score, repair, commit, push, publish, narrate, or begin Movement Five.

(`editor/MOVEMENT-003-REPAIR-REPORT.md`), and a separate same-model informed recheck found
no reason for a second prose pass (`editor/MOVEMENT-003-TARGETED-RECHECK.md`). Where this file and the manuscript ever disagree, the
manuscript governs. Section 0 lists what the repair changed in this record. This file is not manuscript. It carries forward

anything not restated here stands as recorded there.

Purpose: a handoff for the Movement Three targeted recheck and for the Movement Four packet.

## 0. What the 2026-10-11 repair changed in this record

- **Calendar.** The fifth company's Flask is **Wednesday the 29th**, not the 26th. Every
  weekday on the page was already right; only the date number was wrong. Dependent dates
  moved three days: first and second companies Mon 27th, third company Tue 28th, grading
  Thu 30th, the board and standings **Fri the 1st**, the vote **Sat the 2nd**, the Old Yard
  **Sun the 3rd**. The stand-down ends on the **13th**; the tape opens on the **1st of the
  month after**. Scene order and weekdays did not move.
- **Names.** Old Four's Kill is **Gower** (was Tolliver); Old Four's Hold is **Edda** (was
  Merrin); the Kiln's lattice-house warden is **Sedge** (was Tilling). All three were
  author-invented minor names that sat too close to Talley and Perrin by ear.
- **Pronouns.** Grader Halloran is *she*, as in chapter 14. Healer Aske is *he* (three
  uses in chapter 24; the author's choice under the brief's instruction to give a pronoun
  where the prose was avoiding one; easy to reverse).
- **Counts and physical facts corrected:** the crooked post is older than the school (no
  figure); Rhea watched the plate four times, six weeks ago; Jude had four meets left on
  the 13th and heard on Friday that Kiva had run dry; Kiva had laid a cloth on the settling
  floor three times and kept her certificate on three floors and a field; Kellan had met
  the skin twice; the field dram was whole half an hour before the sequence; the walk to
  the table is forty-odd paces; the cords reach the head of the ramp as the count runs
  out; Dunmore saw the lamps from the rim; Old Four's wall stood on the string's south
  side; 51.2 drams makes eight tins and part of a ninth; the New Lane's aisles are three
  paces wide; Rhea Sorn lit seven times at the weir.
- **Knowledge.** Noa no longer knows of Kiva's register entries. He has the keeper's
  remark about the lamps and does not know what a T-0 is. Kiva's disk page says *First
  felt after*, not *Began after*.
- **The dark-floor decision.** The race was raised on the terrace (Gower), Kiva read her
  own half-reading against herself, and Bettan and Vane answered it honestly from
  experience. Anwen's list, Gower, Rhea, Vane, Stroud and one delayed team now carry the
  cost on the page (section 2 and the Pit clock).
- **Chapter 24.** Eight sections (was nine). Mara's deferral is merged into its delivery;
  Orla's remark about the superseded stacks and Rhea's two-word-card teaser are gone from
  this movement; the infirmary healer's account is paid.

### The Return (Sat 18th) — one pass, all numbers agree

Court: the **New Lane**, three hundred years old, behind the school's core house; brick
floor forty paces by twelve; two rows of nine iron-banded oak posts, three paces in from
each long wall and four paces apart (the fourth posts are sixteen paces from the home line,
the fifth posts are the center mark); a six-pace lane between the rows and a three-pace aisle
behind each; no steps, porches or sill. The school copied its Old Yard and copied it
straight: the ninth post stands plumb. Litter dummy: Donny, with an inked mustache. The
same grey-braided Circuit referee as on the 4th.

Rules: Circuit rules exactly as at the Terrace (three carries of four minutes; a down 1;
two halves a down; litter over your own line 3 and the carry ends; at time 1 to the half it
lies in; one dram each for the lane), plus **home rule nine: "A change called to a count is
declared at the count."** As read by the lane warden and the Circuit: Kiva says *Rowan,
changing on three* and the doors; the flag goes up at once; she must change on her own
*three* and at no other instant, or it is undeclared.

| Carry | Kiva declares | Crooked Line | Stonehand | Kiva's dram (cumulative) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Still; no change | 2: Jude downs Arrand; litter in the western half at time. | 3: Keld downs Noa in the north aisle; a second still-door walks round a post and downs Pru while Moss leans on her ward; Jude is closed on by three and downed (after scoring a half on a forearm). Stonehand never contests the litter; Keld "minds" Kiva. | 0.10 |
| 2 | Ember; **one change called to a count (Ember to still)** | 2: Jude downs Moss; Noa's two halves down Arrand. (Kiva's bar, a half on Keld's wrist.) | 4: Brask and Keld lift the litter aside on *two*; Kiva lands on nothing, left foot first; Keld downs her on the brick; litter walked thirty-four paces home. | 0.44 |
| 3 | Still; no change | 5: Pru drops her ward on her own count and Moss falls onto **Kiva's open hand (her first down)**; Kiva opens her stance on a called three and Jude downs Keld; litter over the home line with eleven seconds left. (Jude, a half on Brask.) | 1: Keld downs Noa. (Brask, a half on Jude's arm, off a false push.) | not stated |
| **Final** | one change, called to a count, made on the count; no undeclared change; **no incident** | **9** | **8** | |

Referee's calls, as she states them before the bell and as chapter 15 already used them: **"Half!" names the side that has earned it; "Down!" names the side that has lost a fighter.** In carry three Brask's free palm lands on Jude's forearm behind the blade (half, Stonehand), and later Jude's blade meets Brask's wrist (half, Crooked Line).

The New Lane's aisles are **three paces wide** (posts three paces in from each long wall).

Stonehand's adaptations, in order: (1) leave Kiva the litter and make the other three come
to them among the posts; (2) on hearing a counted change, remove the thing she is coming
at; (3) when the Crooked Line stands still, stand still too, being ahead; (4) a one-handed
push with nothing behind it, to draw Kiva's *now* and catch Jude's arm. The Crooked Line's
corrections: nobody runs, nothing changes, everything is said aloud; the litter goes home
along the south posts four paces at a time, Kiva planting with her right heel on a post's
iron shoe, on the words *Yours* and *Mine*; Pru leans her ward post to post ("a plank") so
it has no ends; Kiva learns to tell an empty push (it stops at her wrists) from a real one
(it reaches the post). The lesson the team carries out, in Kiva's *why* column: "We stopped
feeding him."

Lane card: *Rowan, K. (prov.). Door declared each carry. One change, called to a count
under home rule nine; made on the count; fell. No undeclared change. No incident.* On the
back, the referee repeats her earlier line (a fighter flagged from door to door is
supervised) and adds that Kiva called her own *off* on the floor with nothing lit. She says
aloud that this is not *safe*.

Third team record since the autumn: lost 3–11 (autumn), lost 7–9 (the 4th), lost 6–7 (the
11th), won 9–8 (the 18th). **Three meets remain.**

### The Pit (Wed 29th) — one pass

| Time / count | Event |
|---|---|
| about 10:00 | Fifth company on the floor. Each hand has settled a metered field dram at the keeper's gate. Roles declared; Kiva: *Catch. Door declared: none.* Vane states for the slate that her mark is twenty-two paces from the lattice house and that he has found no cause to unseat her. |
| 10:12 | Kiln opened (keeper's book). The lid goes up; the lattice house lets a Flask through. Eight knots, skinned. |
| | Four student wards make the wall, Anwen in the middle calling the step. All six feet on the sheet. Kellan goes up dark; Kiva says **"Not yet"** (the top knot hangs over the north hem); he comes down dark. Two more steps. "Now." |
| cut | Kellan lights at the top of a bound for about four-tenths of a second. **The skin does not fall and break; it tips and slides down onto the next knot.** Sixty drams fall. |
| count 1–3 | Kiva: "Dark! Whole floor!" Sixteen people put out everything. Corners: Anwen south, Perrin west, Kellan north. |
| in the count | Kiva feels the whole heap "curved," and under it a slow even beat, "one pen on one drum." |
| count 47 | **Sealed.** The third turn of the cord is handed to Anwen on a called three. Kiva reports to Vane: curved, all of it, and "keeping time." He looks at the load board: twelve needles steady. |
| to about count 90 | Perrin carries the bag forty-odd paces to the tinning table in the south-east; Kiva walks with it and does not look back. The blind string walks **ten paces west to the race** and stands astride the gratings. Its lowest knot hangs slack and brushes the bars. |
| past count 90 | Old Four's rung. Their four-ward wall at five paces off its south flank, then Gower's lit blade at the top, cannot turn the reader. Vane: nobody cuts. The skin spreads round the whole column. Cords go down through the gratings to the bus. The twelve load needles go into step; the board's lamps pulse. Kiva feels the beat in the floor. |
| **10:22** | **Vane strikes the event.** "This is a response and it's mine." Students dark and to the east wall. Graywater comes in. A four-Torch wall at five paces reads nothing. Rhea Sorn circles it: "There's no door in it." Greta Stroud slides a flat ward along the gratings to shear the cords; the cords take the ward and the backwash burns both her hands. Vane reassigns Kellan in the field ("You've nothing here to cut. You're my Carry"); Kellan declares Carry and carries her to the west gate. |
| | The string walks south astride the race, a pace about every four counts. Dunmore leaves for his road door: four minutes. Vane sends every still-door to the capstan (Brask, Cade, two more, and the Hall's four on duty). |
| | Kiva sees the cords running ahead in the race: a grating every four counts, **fifteen gratings from the sump, sixty counts.** She calls it to Vane. "Who's south of it?" "Rowan. Seven. Catcher." |
| **10:23** | She states that it wants a still-door on the shelf, that the only way over is a Stride, that she has both, that it is not on her paper, and that she has missed fourteen of fourteen. Orla, at the east floor rail, refuses to certify, says what she has seen, and says "you write it as mine." **Vane's field direction:** one attempt, door to door, declared; Dray to follow and pin. **In the same breath he records his own trade:** the lid is not down, he had said the lid first, and he is opening the link with the doorway two-thirds clear, knowing the lattice will drop: "Pumps before doorway," eight still-doors on the bar, "the foreman's trade, and it's on the slate as his." Kiva, told three times what the lever does to the lattice, has not thought of it since she began counting. |
| about 8 counts left | Her count of iron runs 48, 44, 40, 36 through the exchange, 22 and 16 on the thirteen paces, and 8 at the lip. Thirteen paces to the lip, a pace east of the race. "Rowan, changing on three! Ember, to still!" Vane raises his chalk hand as a flag. |
| *One* | Struts; she leaves the lip. |
| *Two* | Ember shut at the top; the metal home; nothing open. |
| *Three* | **The still door set under her before the shelf; right foot down into it. No jar; the left knee feels nothing.** |
| *Four* | Right fist and left elbow on the crossbar. She lets her weight go down. |
| *Five* | The lever comes down a quarter turn; the blade lifts. **Every lamp out; the lattice off.** The lever is a thumb's width short of level. |
| *Six* | Her count of iron stands at one. The cords have come to the end of the race and lie at the head of the ramp, three paces below; they stop and let go, and spill into the sump as loose ash. |
| *Seven* | The shine goes off the string and gathers on one face. The reader finds the nearest worked thing, her stance, and turns to her; one leg comes a pace nearer the sump. |
| *Eight, nine* | The Ember and the hand door stir with fear; she gives each a number and keeps both shut. |
| | Selka Dray lands on the last half pace of shelf, hangs her weight on the crossbar, and drops the pin through the lower eye. "Mine. Let go." |
| *Ten, eleven* | Three Graywater wards come to ten paces and take the face. **Kiva gives the wall its weight back and shuts the still door.** The Flask walks north. |
| 10:24 | Link opened from the floor (keeper's book). |
| after | She finds a third of the dram gone, the three lines aching and not burned, the left arm slack, the first two fingers white, her nose bleeding. **The disk is warm.** She closes her fist on it and asks it nothing. |
| about 40 s after the link | **The fragment**, three or four counts, standing. Dray hauls her to the wall by belt and collar, counting for her. Dray: the lamps on the dead ring "jumped," all of them, once. Keeper's book: *T-0.* |
| lid | Perrin cutaway. The lid is a third down when the lamps go. A **second string's** legs come under it and stop it. Eight still-doors plant. Brask calls the Crooked Line's count: *give* on three, then *take*; the lid comes down on the off-balance leg and seats. A severed length of leg pours on the open floor: sweepings. |
| kill | Rhea cutaway. Rung two on Old Four's sheet, on their catcher's word. Three, four (Anwen's ten-count relief behind Imrie, no join heard), five. The skin slides down a rung each time and thickens; the sixth is found by ear and takes a full second of light. **Seven and eight with one level line**, broadside, on a double sheet, on Bettan's word, about a second and a half. |
| +4 min 9 s | Dunmore comes through his door and padlocks the pin. Sixty-one steps up the road stair. The disk is cold by the keeper's gate. |
| inside the hour | Kiva's bag is panned at the table by Anwen and Kellan with Perrin cutting cords, on Kiva's calls; eight tins and part of a ninth; **51.2 drams** on Bettan's balance. Gower stops at the table (section 2). Aske eases the arm at the Wren House cart on a signed page. |
| 4 p.m. | Branch restored under a Home Line wall; the lattice "took back what was behind" the lid. The Pit is shut for a fortnight. |

Vane's slate: *T7. Rung one: hold, cut, bag sealed at 47. 51.2 on the table, to grade.
Nothing printed. Co. left no gate lit; string took the race; event struck 10:22. Field
direction 10:23: Rowan to S. link, two doors in sequence, declared. Link ordered open with
lid a third down: foreman's trade, pumps before doorway. Link opened; held till pinned
(Dray). Lattice dropped; second string started; lid seated on it (Brask, eight on the
bar). Hurt: Rowan, collapse after. Cade, ribs, again. Rung passed, subject to the bag. The
rest is for the board.*

The company slate of Thu the 23rd now reads: *5th Co. No gate left lit during counts.
Whole floor dark, catcher's word. Reason given: wind-borne foul at Sag Line; floor falls
south; "nothing to lean at." Race raised (Gower); answered, no pattern in a bar (Bettan;
foreman agrees). Foreman would leave a gate. Not forbidden.*

Whole Flask: **419 of 480 drams tinned** (board's figure). Pump house (Mara, standing in
it): the bank of twenty-four went into step at 10:22 and came out at 10:24; no cell
fouled; the pumps never dropped a stroke.

**The Pit's sensing-mast tape, Kiva's pen** (Noa's reading; one pen per fighter, not per
door): flat from the gate until nearly 10:24; a step of **0.02** (the bound); then **0.26
in a fifth of a second**, straight up, with a blot at the top, at the moment when by every
account she had nothing open; then **0.03** over a little more than nine seconds (the
stance on the lever). **Total 0.31 dram. Noa works the Draw at "near twelve."**

## 2. Character state at exit

**Kiva Rowan.** Provisional Third Division, Crooked Line third team. **Ysra's conditions
unchanged. Orla's certificate unchanged: recovery only, dark hands.** No incident on her
file.
- **The sequence.** One correct deliberate two-door sequence, Stride into stance, on the
  29th, under a field direction and outside her certificate. About nine seconds of stance.
  Fourteen recorded misses before it (tries 1–14 as in the calendar; the tenth was in a
  sanctioned lane against her sponsor's instruction). It has not been repeated. Orla has
  seen it once and will not sign: **"Show me six."**
- What she now knows about it: a drop and a jam; that leaning one door into the other is
  still a jam; that the Ember can be shut at the top of the bound because a Stride is paid
  for at take-off; that between the doors the speed is in her body and in neither door;
  that the stance must be set before the ground, as a pan is set before the ash; that the
  speed goes into the ground through her and is not a blow to spend on an opponent or a
  lever; that being pleased opens a door as surely as being frightened, so she must count
  through it. What she does not know: what carries the work across the gap, or why the
  gap costs more than both doors.
- Skills gained: bagging on a purse sheet; calling a cord check; handing a task across on
  called words (*Yours, Mine,* a counted three); home rule nine; reading an empty push;
  the open hand (her first down in a sanctioned lane); the bar as a gate against open
  hands; shutting the Ember at the top of each bound.
- Her mistakes, in her own book: she called the whole floor dark; the foreman said he
  would leave a gate and she did not; Anwen asked why they leave one, twice, and she had
  her answer ready both times; Gower asked about the race and she was glad when her own
  half-reading was answered; she set the mark by the loudest pattern and never asked which
  was nearest; she walked off with her own bag and did not watch the string. Under a
  separate heading, *The link, before the lid*, she records the foreman's trade and reason
  as his, and as hers that she had been told three times what the lever does, did not
  think of it, and would have gone anyway. Earlier: she broke her word to Orla in the lane.
- Costs she has written: a branch for six hours; the Pit for a fortnight; eleven teams
  their day ("I've met one"); Gower's rung; 0.31 of a dram; the sealed dram. Under *How
  long to come back* she writes, of Anwen, "I don't know."
- **Injuries.** Left knee: re-injured on the 18th ("the size of a small loaf"), strapped;
  no landings until the 2nd of next month by the infirmary healer's first ruling. She
  bounded once on the 29th inside that ban and landed without harm; **the healer heard,
  sent Orla an account (strapping twice, one opinion not taken, one further fortnight), and
  Orla paid it.** Kiva repays her by carrying the crate until the 13th. Left first and second fingers: unchanged through the 28th
  (slow, late; she lost a cord turn in them on the 10th and hooks the left elbow instead of
  trusting them); bone white and nearly dead on the lever on the 29th; **eased by Healer
  Aske with ward-trace ash inside the hour. Aske: not burned, "starved"; they will sulk a
  week and then be where they were on the 28th, "slow, and late, and not finished mending.
  No better."** Had she come the next day, the verdict would have been worse. **Permanent
  loss remains the Movement Five cost.** Heat-lines: all three ached their whole length
  after the sequence, "a muscle used for the first time"; not burned; the bottom line lit
  only in jams (tries 2, 4, 5, 8, 14). Nosebleed after the sequence, and again at the cart
  when she nearly laughed. Fever on the night of the 29th, a slow heat and a headache, no dream.
  **Stood down a fortnight from the 29th, to the 13th: no lanes, no floors, nothing lit,
  no landings.**
- **The disk.** Cold on the 7th and the 28th. **Warm once, on the 29th:** she **first felt it after**
  she had shut the still door with the lever on its pin, when her fingers went to the
  lines; she does not know when it began, and her page says so (she wrote *Began*, crossed
  it out, and wrote *First felt*); "warm as a hand";
  it lasted from the shelf to the top of the road stair and was cold by the keeper's
  gate. She asked it nothing; it did nothing. Kennel ash is still in three grooves. Selka
  Dray saw her holding something at her collar and was told only "a thing I wear. It's
  warm. It isn't, generally." Rhea Sorn saw the fist at the collar from across the floor.
  Orla and Mara have been told. Orla did not put out a hand to feel it.
- **The dream.** No recurrence in sleep. **One waking fragment on the 29th**, three or
  four counts, standing on the shelf with the disk warm in her fist: the height; the
  lights coming by every road; **new: each light has its own note, none in tune; she
  tries to count them and cannot keep a number past four;** the lights touch and the
  notes become one note; no sky; what is above comes down; **new: below, something small
  and exact answers, "tick."** Afterward the one note stays with her and she cannot hum
  it. She reported it to Mara the same night; Mara wrote it down, including that "tick"
  is "the word she'd use for both" the bag and the dream. The name Unroofed Sky is not on
  the page.
- Papers in her field book: Dunmore's T-0 line for the 29th; the Hanley Street copy of
  the 2446 entry with its drawn margin. She has kept both from the team, and has told
  Mara that she will ask the Compact Library's reader herself, alone first, once Aske
  lets her travel. The four lines Halloran gave her for returning ash at par.

**Mara Rowan.** Examiner Rusk's ruling on the 9th: **Grade Two tuning license suspended to
the autumn sitting, not revoked;** she re-sits the practical in the ninth month with **the
left entered as her reading hand;** until then she tunes, refills and seals nothing; the
flat panel stays quenched and tagged; **a fine of one week's fees** for three years of an
undeclared hand (Rusk chose the smallest week in her books, "the one with the flood");
repair and reconciliation continue; and Rusk gives her the **baseline books for every Sag
Line mast and every drum on the Brickfields trunk** to go through, at records rate, "about
half of what the tuning did." She declared the numb right palm herself when Rusk noticed
her reading a bench cell left-handed; he had guessed from her handwriting on cell tags. He
credits the mast six entry as "a habit, and a good one," and her filing against herself on
the 13th. Trunk book: cell nineteen's baseline 0.14 (2674), 0.15, 0.17, 0.18 this spring.
She has written in the margin that the Pit's south link should be boxed and a second
fitted at the Kiln end. On the 29th she stood in the pump house with her hand on cell
nine. She told Kiva on the 28th that she could sit in a gallery now. She had the keeper take her through his door to the south link on Tue the 21st. She has had the keeper's branch book since the night of the 29th and read its T-0 line three times before showing it on the 2nd. Her acceptance,
stated on the night of the 29th: Kiva telling a Home Line officer what she could not do
and then being let do it by someone whose business it was "is a meter, and a shutdown
path, and a book." She still checks the quenched hall panel.

**Orla Dane.** Set and watched all fourteen yard and lane tries; came to the Low Lane on
the 13th for the first time in anyone's memory. Forbade the fast count in a lane; after
Kiva did it anyway, told her that her standing was not Kiva's to spend without asking,
shut the yard until a healer had seen the knee, and then gave her the lesson she had paid
for. At the Pit she left her seat when the board began to pulse, refused to certify what
she had not seen, said what she had seen, and took the consequence on herself aloud. She
has added nothing to the certificate. She says there is a **bearer's book at the Home Line
hall**, that she has known of it thirty years and never been on its list, that Ilse Corran
was on the list and she thinks wrote in it, and that Hollis Vane is on it as watch officer.
She asked Vane on the 30th for a favor saved "twenty-five years." At the cart on the 29th
she told Kiva to bring the page on Sunday and she should have what Orla had; on the 3rd
she gave it (the bearer's book, the list, Vane's ledger). **After the repair she says
nothing in this movement about the superseded stacks.** She paid the infirmary healer's
account. She says of the crossed-out word on Kiva's page, "I'd have let that one by. I'd
have been wrong to."

**Anwen Pryce.** Graywater shadow rotation continuing; inside the tape at the weir, behind
Greta Stroud. Hold for Team Seven's rung on the 29th, calling the step for a four-ward
student wall; took the cord's third turn on a called three. In the response, placed by
Mace behind Imrie's shoulder, not in the line; **took the wall for ten counts on rung four
and gave it back; neither Rhea nor Mace heard the join.** Read Kiva's chest and hand and
pressed for the same-hour easing. **Mace's summer line: offered on the 28th, accepted on
the morning of the 2nd; six weeks from the 10th of next month, the culverts and the Sag,
reliefs and lamps, with a stipend.** She will miss the school's summer card and whatever
Team Seven does in those weeks, and says so at the table. Voted for the two-door ration
on three written conditions; Kiva reads each back to her and rewrites the third.
**Her list:** *Tues., Kennel. Asked about the gate. Answered with the Sag.* and *Thurs.,
the Pit. Asked why they leave one. Nobody could say. Put my hand up.* She shows it to
Kiva at the vote, says she will not be let off her own hand and will not let Kiva off
either, that a dram at par "doesn't do it," and that "not having your answer ready would
be a start." She votes for the dram. Nothing between them is settled.

**Perrin Cade.** Ribs strapped throughout; on the Hall's lid duty on the 27th and 28th and
knows the capstan. Sat with his Hall at the Return. Gave Kiva *arrive before you're
there*. On the 29th: a corner, the bag carried on a pole across his shoulders, then the
capstan under Brask; **the two cracked ribs "will have to begin again"** (strapped from
armpits to hips on the 30th). Brask told him first, and promised to carry him to the
healer. At the table Vane says the ribs are in his sum "under my name"; Perrin answers,
"Brask told me first. Before he asked. I'd recommend it." **The Hall's master has his name down for the second team in the autumn on
Brask's word; he is to try the plate again when he can breathe.** Says he minds the two
drams for Kiva's yard and votes yes.

**Kellan Renn.** Supplied the missing half of the sequence without knowing it was unusual
(a Stride is a push; shut it at the top). Timed Rhea Sorn at the weir: under six seconds of
light in eleven minutes twenty. A week in the high lane: goes up dark, lights for about
four-tenths at the top, comes down dark. Asked his catcher; spent a bound dark on "not
yet." Cut rung one. Declared Carry and carried Stroud out with nothing lit. Panned the
left pan badly. **A Halden-sponsored license team for the Regional has offered him a place
and a stipend; he has said only that he will compare, and has told the team that he is
"doing the sum."** He put the two-dram ration on his own list.

**Noa Bexley.** Sanctioned readings continue. Sat at the Pit's mast as the Chair's reader.
Wrote the tape's figures into Kiva's book and his own. **Refused the Halden technical
office:** "They're her numbers… I measure. I don't own." His page of things that are
probably nothing now also holds a plate in a meadow, a seventh reed, and the T-0. Last
line: "They can look now. So I'd better find out first." Kiva's monthly consent falls due
for renewal early next month; not shown.

**Jude Talley.** Third-year; first step, no license team (his broke up two years ago).
Declined to lend Kiva his ration and said he had looked at it properly. Captain's plan
for the Return and its correction. **Second step, full, on the 28th** as a reserve blade in
the third company. Tells Kiva he will not be her captain in the autumn and does not know
what he will be.

**Pru Finch.** First step on the 14th. Her ward hums when leaned on wood. Called "Gate on
three" and dropped her wall on her own count.

**Lowen Brask.** Stonehand third team captain; **Carry on license team "Old Four"** (Team
Four of 2678; 20.6 drams two springs ago; eighteenth by yield). Hold 7.5, stalled for two
springs; not strong enough for the Hall's plates; teaches the junior lanes. Read the
Crooked Line home book. In the lane he saw that Kiva's legs were bare of metal when she
landed and that one door, the right one, opened late; he told her so, and asked where the
speed had been meant to go. Supported whole floor dark on the terrace. **Commanded the
eight on the Kiln's capstan and used a counted give-and-take to seat the lid on a second
string.** **Old Four is attached to the Home Line at Vane's request; Vane has offered him a
summer on a hold crew.**

**Selka Dray.** Reserve blade for qualifier week. Objected to Kiva's seat on evidence she
went and found (the Pit runs off the Ferren pump bank). Told she might renew on evidence.
On the 29th she vaulted the rail on Vane's order, crossed the sump on her struts, hung on
the lever to level it, pinned it, hauled Kiva back from the edge and kept her count. She knows whose notion the dark floor was because she heard Kiva call it from
the rail and the reserves told her. **She
has not changed her mind, says so, and has renewed the objection: Kiva was on a floor and
a branch went dark, and the dark floor was Kiva's notion. Ysra has not ruled** and will
hear it with the tape. Team Nineteen's status is still not stated.

**Ysra Crest.** Read the board's findings on the 1st (section 6). Left Kiva's place,
conditions and certificate as they were.

**Rhea Sorn.** Cut the weir Flask and seven rungs at the Pit. Has not spoken to Kiva.
Third draft card: *Flask, the Pit, the 29th. Company left no gate; string took the bus.
Rowan opened the south link on two doors, one after the other. Nothing dropped between.
About ten seconds. Then down. That is not what I saw on the plate. Still NOT for draft.
Attach Seven to Graywater. I'll take the shift myself.* The "Corran's disk" card is not
mentioned in this movement after the repair; it stands as Movement Two left it, private.
**She owns the ward slide in her own viewpoint** ("That was Rhea's, and she did not dress
it") and walks to the stretcher after the last rung to say so; Stroud confirms it in the
cart. She watched the trial plate four times, six weeks ago. Memory added: in the
culvert, Ilse Corran held her with one arm and gripped something at her own throat with
the other hand.

**Hollis Vane.** Foreman of the fifth company. Told the company he would leave a gate and
that it was theirs to call; agreed with Bettan that a string does not notice a bar; wrote
down their reason and the answered objection. **Owns, aloud and on the slate, the trade of
opening the link with the lid a third down** ("Pumps before doorway"; "the line under it
is mine alone"; he would weigh it the same way again; Cade's ribs are in it under his
name). Asked that his own name stand first
in the board's paragraph. Per Orla: on the bearer's book list; went to write his reason
for the field direction "in a ledger older than this school" and "found that somebody had
been there before him"; had been about to come and ask Orla. Nothing more is on the page.

## 3. New people and places

People (pronouns as written; all are the author's choice unless noted):
- **Examiner Rusk** (he; about sixty; clerk's cuff guards, operator's thumbs; "I don't
  average"). Chapter 10 already calls him "a man called Rusk."
- **Keeper Dunmore** (he; initials A.D.; Works; has kept the Pit's branch since 2659;
  lost his second man in 2671).
- **Warden Sedge** (she; the Kiln's lattice house; heard, not seen). Renamed in the repair (was Tilling).
- Graywater: **Greta Stroud** (ward line, about thirty, broken nose; both hands burned,
  six weeks); **Imrie** (ward line; she); a square, silent catcher (he; unnamed).
- Old Four: **Gower** (Kill; lanky Crooked Line third-year with a sardonic mouth; raised
  the race on the terrace, voted against the dark floor "on a dislike," lit his blade to be
  looked at, and lost his rung to Rhea Sorn's blade: "It doesn't seem to be a thing I can
  use"; renamed in the repair, was Tolliver); **Edda** (Hold; small, stern, Wren House;
  renamed, was Merrin); a quiet catcher (he; unnamed).
- **Bettan** (Home Line; she): has knelt behind strings over more copper than the Pit's and
  never saw one notice a bar; "I'll swear to that much, and not to what I haven't seen."
- A thin, older Wren House boy, **catcher of Team Twenty-Eight** (sixth company, due at two
  o'clock on the 29th; unnamed). His father shut the shop to watch. He did not sign Dray's
  paper and has since written to the Chair by himself.
- Stonehand third team's first-year is **Arrand**.
- Unnamed: the school infirmary healer (she; above); a man from the **Halden technical office**
  (about forty; black coat, gold cord); Monday's foremen (a Saltmere officer, a Halden
  man); a Circuit clerk at the tinning table; a one-eyed woman who taught Brask's junior
  lanes.
- **Grader Halloran** is *she* (as in chapter 14). **Healer Aske** is *he* in chapter 24 (author's choice in the repair; chapters 1–16 use no pronoun). The school infirmary healer is a stout, unhurried woman with ink on her cuff who writes the whole time she talks.

Places:
- **The Pit** (the Circuit's arena off the Brickfields road): an old clay pit faced in
  yellow brick; oval; twelve terraces; eleven hundred seats; forty lamps on the rim;
  cinder floor **seventy paces north to south and forty across**, falling a little to the
  south, so that cold air creeps that way until the sun reaches the west terrace.
  North: **the Kiln**, a brick beehive twice the height of a house, with a cart-wide
  doorway closed by **the lid** (a two-hundred-year-old cast-iron slab with a ward pattern
  in its face, hung on chains, raised and lowered by a four-bar capstan on a stone
  platform; two minutes with four still-doors, ten with eight ordinary men) and the
  **lattice house** against its flank. Down the middle: **the race**, a line of pace-wide
  iron gratings over a brick channel carrying **two bare copper bus bars**. South: a sheer
  wall four times a man's height, with the road above; at its foot **the sump**, a trench
  three paces wide and a man and a half deep, where the bars come out on a ramp and enter
  a tunnel. On the wall beyond the sump: **the south link**, a copper blade in two jaws
  with a man-long sprung lever and a crossbar handle, above an **iron shelf** a pace deep
  and four long; **reached only by the keeper's iron door** from a sixty-one-step road
  stair. East wall: **the load board** (twelve dials in a glass case) under the great
  board, the keeper's gate, the reserves' rail, the sensing mast on the rim (sixteen pens)
  with a mast room beneath. South-east: the tinning table, five paces short of the sump
  and fourteen east of the race. West: the Graywater gate and the Wren House cart.
- **Ferren pump house**, across the Brickfields road: a bank of twenty-four cells and
  three pumps that keep the Ferren low streets dry. **The Pit has no cell of its own;** its
  branch leaves the pump bank's trunk through the one link. In the trunk book: *S. link.
  Pinned and locked, 2661.* The keeper draws the lock at dawn on any day the Kiln is
  opened and stands by at the load board, four minutes from the lever by road.
- **Ferren weir meadow** and the buried feeder to the weir's sluice motors (laid 2588,
  from the same bank, with flush iron inspection plates every twenty paces).
- **The New Lane** (above). **The Shallows** (Wren House's Third Division court, a tiled
  floor under a hand's depth of water). The Works' examiners' room.

All new names were searched for in this book's manuscript and provenance,
`books/book-01-kindled`, `books/book-02-meridian` and `universe/` before use; none matched.

## 4. World facts established on the page

- **Practice ash.** Third Division draws **half a dram a week** from the Low Lane store, on
  Mondays, in a wax-stoppered glass the size of a thimble. The three-door drill costs "a
  hundredth and a bit." A drop cost 0.04 and a jam 0.09 on the Low Lane mast.
- **The Flask** (Druce): twenty Handfuls in the yield book, **480 drams.** The ash does not
  make one large knot; it makes **eight, one above another on one cord: "a string."** Each
  knot is about **sixty drams** and lends its own limbs; the whole stands about as high as
  a tram's roof on six thick legs, the lowest two knots down among the legs. **The top
  knot reads**, out to twenty or twenty-five paces; the others walk where it points. Cut
  the top knot and the string is **blind for "a slow hundred"** (Druce has seen eighty)
  until the next knot wakes. The yield book calls a knot **a rung.** Rungs are cut from
  the top, one at a time, each caught before the next. The last two carry the legs and are
  cut together, or "it sits down wherever it happens to be" (**the sit-down**). "A Handful's
  a fight… A Flask is a schedule."
- **Purse sheet:** waxed canvas six paces square, lead in the hem, a cord through the hem.
  The catcher sets and calls it; three more take corners; the neck is hauled shut, three
  turns and a wax seal. **Bagged inside the count is tinned, for the book;** the bag is
  carried off dark and panned at a table **within the hour.** What is tinned on a bought
  Kennel floor goes back on the Kennel's shelf.
- **Company:** four teams, sixteen hands, one chartered foreman. Each team takes one rung
  whole (hold, cut, bag, carry) in yield order while the other three mind the string; four
  wards make the wall for every rung, the cutting team's ward in the middle calling the
  step; the foreman's crew takes the bottom four. **Mark: fifty of the sixty bagged inside
  the count at Grade Second or better, nothing printed, nothing fouled, nobody hurt,** and
  the foreman's four columns for the company. Thirty-one teams; companies qualified two a
  day from Monday the 27th; a chartered company supplies the safety crew by the day
  (Graywater had Wednesday). Reserves carry yellow cards and are seated when a team is
  short.
- **A gate.** Chartered crews leave a wall lit ten or twelve paces off through every
  count, and a blind string walks to it and leans (Bettan: "It likes a gate. Always leave
  it a gate."). It was a custom and not in the Circuit's book. **As of the night of the
  30th it is a rule: "A gate shall be left lit."**
- **Professional practice at the weir:** eight purse sheets laid in a row, one a rung; the
  ward line walks the face down the row; the blade asks the catcher before every cut and
  waits for the word. Rhea Sorn, with no Stride, stands on the ground and lets her blade
  be as long as the cut needs for about seven-tenths of a second. Student blades go up the
  back of the string on their struts and light at the top of a bound.
- **Graywater relief:** the new holder brings her wall up inside the old one; the old one
  leans on it until hers holds nothing, then lets go. "If you can hear the join, it's a
  bad one." A ward-holder stands no more than four hours.
- **The south link** wants about **four hundredweight** to bring the lever level for its
  pin: "one good still-door with his boot on the shelf, or two big men." It is sprung to
  shut. The breaker-and-quench distinction from Movement One stands; opening the link
  drops the whole branch, including the Kiln's lattice.
- **The comb:** the seventh reed, one of the four unnamed, is carded on the reference
  shelf as **I, for induction.** Halloran had heard it twice in nineteen years, both times
  for ash swept from a cell that had been salted late: "A tune leaves that. Something
  that's been made to keep step."
- **Second step, attached** (new): a team may answer a Flask-yield call only as an
  attached recovery and support team under a chartered crew until it has stood one whole
  Flask under a foreman without remark.
- **Circuit tapes** are open to chartered parties thirty days after the standings post.
- Ash office posted price added: **purse sheet and poles, 3.0.**
- **Home rule nine** and the Home Line "reasons" practice in a field direction, as above.

## 5. The spawn expression (shown, not explained)

What the page shows, in order:
1. At the weir, on an ordinary skinned Flask, the skin fell and broke on the sheet at each
   rung. After rung five the blind string stopped for two or three counts on an iron
   inspection plate over the sluice feeder, its lowest knot brushing the iron, and then
   went on to the lit wall. Kiva wrote it down with a question mark; Bettan noticed and
   said nothing. Whether the feeder was live is not known.
2. At the Pit, the skin **did not break when the top knot was cut. It slid down onto the
   next knot**, and did so again at every later rung, thickening each time until it lay
   over the last two knots "like a lid on a pan."
3. Rung-one ash was "curved" throughout, not in a few grains, and Kiva felt in it a slow
   even beat. It graded **First** (the test tune held 118 counts) and read **W, full; I,
   trace.** Halloran: it was bagged before the string went near the race, so "it brought
   that with it."
4. Left blind with **nothing lit on the floor**, the string did not go to the loudest
   pattern (the Kiln's lattice, twenty-two paces from the mark). It went to the **nearest**
   (the bus, ten paces) and stood astride it.
5. When the next knot woke, it would not turn to a four-ward wall at five paces or to a
   lit blade at arm's length. The skin closed round the whole column, so that no edge was
   left to cut behind. **Cords** went down through the gratings onto the bus. Every load
   on the branch went into step. A ward laid across the cords was taken in, as open ash
   takes a pattern, and burned its holder.
6. It then **walked the bus** toward the tunnel and the pump bank, the cords running
   ahead of the legs at about a grating every four counts.
7. When the link was opened and the branch went dead, the cords stopped, let go, and
   became loose ash; the shine withdrew to one face; and the top knot read again, turning
   first to the nearest worked thing (Kiva's stance) and then to a brighter wall.
8. With the lattice dead and the lid a third down, **a second string began to come
   through the Kiln.** The lid was seated on it. When the branch was restored the lattice
   "took back what was behind it."
9. Forty seconds or so after the link opened, every lamp on the isolated ring "jumped"
   once. The keeper entered **T-0.** This coincided with the disk's warmth and the
   fragment. **Nobody on the page connects these.**

Not shown and not claimed: any cause; any link to the filter; any link between the skin,
the strong class, the creeping baselines, and the T-0 entries. Rhea Sorn's reading on the
floor ("it's found something better") is her judgment in the moment. The Home Line's
phrase "a dirty spring" stands. Wren House has carded the reed and drawn no conclusion.

## 7. The board's findings (read by Ysra, Fri 1st)

- *Was it closed:* the Kiln is on its lid; branch restored at four under a Home Line wall;
  the Pit shut a fortnight. *Who was hurt:* a chartered ward-holder, both hands, six
  weeks; two students. *What still works:* the Ferren pumps. *What was tinned:* 419 drams,
  of which 51.2 by Team Seven before anything went wrong.
- The fifth company left no gate lit; that was inside the Circuit's book and outside the
  Home Line's custom; the foreman said he would leave one and recorded it; neither book
  nor custom foresaw a string that could take a bus. Vane's name stands first in that
  paragraph at his own request. The new rule follows.
- **Second step, attached**, for each team of the fifth company. **Old Four to the Home
  Line** (Vane asked for them). **Team Seven to the Graywater Company**, asked for by name
  by Rhea Sorn, "I'll take the shift myself."
- Eleven teams (the sixth to eighth companies) lost their day and will wait for a held
  tear; several have written to the Chair. Selka Dray has renewed her objection on
  evidence; not ruled.
- Kiva: the event was struck at 10:22 and the field direction given at 10:23 with a
  reason; nothing that followed was in a sanctioned event; **no incident.** Place,
  conditions and certificate unchanged. Ysra notes that the act cost more ash for its
  length than anything on that mast's drum.

## 9. Items for owner or editorial attention

Routes the author chose where the packet left room, and places where the draft may have
gone further than intended:

1. **The sequence is used for infrastructure, not recovery, and is outside her
   certificate.** The page handles it by a struck event, a recorded field direction, a
   declared change, and a sponsor who refuses to certify and takes the consequence. If the
   owner wants the first sequence to sit inside the certificate, this is the scene to
   change.
2. **Lowen Brask is not at the lever.** He commands the lid. The second hand at the link is
   Selka Dray, by design: it gives her an act without softening her objection. An earlier
   plan had Brask relieve Kiva at the lever; the shelf's geometry (no still-door can cross)
   made that false.
3. **Kiva bounds on a knee under a healer's ban**, eleven days after the injury. The correct
   landing is what spares it. After the repair the infirmary healer sends her account to
   Orla, who pays it; the fortnight is extended to the 13th.
4. **The fragment comes while she is awake**, standing, with the disk warm in her fist.
   The bible describes a fever dream that "recurs in pieces"; this is a new form of it.
   The fever follows that night with no dream.
5. **The T-0 on a dead lamp ring** is new evidence the author introduced. It coincides
   with the disk and the fragment and is left unconnected. It strengthens the Movement One
   register plant; it also narrows what Movement Four can say the T-0s are.
6. **A second string starting through the Kiln** when the lattice dropped is the closure
   stake for the lid crew. It implies the bottle-rift can hold more than it is asked for.
7. **The seventh reed, "I" for induction,** and Grade First for a student rung.
8. **"Second step, attached,"** the new gate rule, and the thirty-day rule for tapes are
   institutional inventions of this draft.
9. **Hollis Vane's ledger and the bearer's book** are reported by Orla only. "Somebody had
   been there before him" is deliberately undefined; Movement Four must decide who.
10. **Orla's remark about the superseded stacks was removed in the repair.** Kiva still
    does not know Annick exists. The stacks are named once, by Mara, as where the 2446
    margin can be read.
11. **Kiva breaks her word to her sponsor** in chapter 19. It is the movement's sharpest
    fault in her, and it is answered by Orla and paid for in the knee, not excused.
12. The team's error at the Pit rests on a lesson from the meet ("we stopped feeding
    him") and on Druce's and Vane's shared expectation that a blind string goes to the
    loudest pattern. Vane's caution is on the page twice. Review should check that the
    foreman does not read as negligent.
13. **Whole Flask yield** is 419 of 480; only Team Seven's 51.2 is traced through the
    shares. The other three teams' credited recovery is not stated.
14. The Wren House meet of the 11th is one 280-word section. The packet warns against
    montage; this is the only summarized contest.
15. **Secondary viewpoint after the repair is about 12.4%** by the editorial script's
    word count (six people: Rhea 1,580, Mara 1,058, Noa 975, Perrin 968, Brask 866, Anwen
    603); Kiva holds 87.55%. No viewpoint was added. **Scene sections average about 838
    words** (58 sections) against the ~950 proxy. **Chapter 22 is 3,961 words;** chapters
    22 and 23 are one continuous action and the break was kept where it is.
16. **Movement length after the repair is 48,693 words** (`wc -w`; first draft 45,009),
    inside the 45,000–50,000 working range. **Chapter 24 grew from 6,319 to 8,571 words**
    because most of the costs the brief asked to be paid between people are paid there.
    The owner may want it trimmed; see the repair report.
17. The formula's numerical targets were measured by the editorial review and again after
    the repair (`AUTHORSHIP.md`). Whole-text targets are still missed. Narration alone
    moved from a 10.85-word mean and 0.78% forty-word sentences to 12.03 and 2.00%; in the
    four chapters without a set piece (17, 18, 21, 24) it is 12.6 to 13.1.
18. Pronouns chosen by the author: Dunmore, Gower, Healer Aske and the Halden man he;
    Sedge, Stroud, Imrie, Edda and the infirmary healer she. Halloran is she, from
    chapter 14.
19. The author did not re-read the prologue or most of Movements One and Two in this
    session (see `AUTHORSHIP.md`). Small facts taken from the checkpoints should be
    checked against the manuscript: the grey-braided referee's manner, Druce's "hm" (used
    three times here), Anwen's "I want it said" and "It's said" (used by Anwen, Perrin, Kiva
    and Brask), and the settling of a dram.
20. Elapsed-time phrases worth a mechanical check: "a month ago" (several), "six weeks
    ago" (Mara's embankment paper; Rhea's four viewings of the plate), "eleven hundred
    people," "four minutes and nine seconds," "seventeen days ago" (the weir, from the
    Pit), "a week ago today" (Mara at the link), "eleven days after" (lane to Pit).

## 10. Deliberately not done

The disk does not teach, speak, answer a question, grant power, or warm on command; it
warms once, after. No second sequence is attempted. No two doors are held open together
on purpose, and no three. No rank changes; no hold or bench figure is re-measured. The
finger dullness is neither cured nor made permanent. The Unroofed Sky is not named and
gives no tactic. The cause of the Fall, the filter theory, the disk's origin and the
Makers are not explained. No character connects the skin, the strong class, the
baselines, the T-0 entries and the disk. Rhea Sorn does not speak to Kiva. Hollis Vane
does not see or mention the disk. Lowen Brask is not made cruel and Selka Dray is not
made a friend. The students kill one rung of a Flask and no more. In the repair: no
event, tactic, score, yield, share, ruling or clock time was changed; chapters 22 and 23
were not shortened; no viewpoint was added; the board's findings were left word for word.
The repair was not reviewed or rechecked by its author, and nothing was committed or
pushed. Movement Four has not been started.

## 11. What Movement Four opens on

State on the evening of Sunday the 3rd:

- Kiva has, in her field book: one page recording the disk's warmth with no guess on it;
  Mara's page of the fragment; a keeper's T-0 line four days old; and a clerk's copy of
  the 2446 entry with a margin she cannot read. She has written, at the foot of the page,
  *Six. And find out who wrote it down.*
- Three inquiries are already moving, by three people, for three reasons: **Noa** toward
  the 0.26 that went out of her with nothing open, with thirty days before any chartered
  party may read the same tape, and toward what a T-0 is; **Orla** toward the bearer's
  book; **Hollis Vane** toward an earlier hand in a Home Line ledger, which he cannot yet
  read or date. Kiva herself means to take the 2446 margin to the Library's reader.
- Unsettled between people: Anwen and Kiva; Gower; Greta Stroud ("I'd not hurry me");
  Team Twenty-Eight's catcher and ten other teams; Selka Dray.
- Public record now holds: a sanctioned tape with an act on it that nobody can classify;
  a Wren House card reading W full, I trace; a draft card asking for Team Seven by name;
  a board finding and a new rule; a renewed objection. The anomaly is searchable by
  anyone with a charter, and Halden has begun to look.
- Team Seven is attached to Graywater under Rhea Sorn's own supervision, ninth by yield,
  with 2.0 sealed, 1.6 held, a sheet of its own, and six funded tries. Anwen leaves for
  six weeks on the 10th. Kellan is weighing another team. Perrin is strapped and bound
  for his Hall's second team. Kiva cannot light anything or land until the 13th.
- Withheld for Movement Four and after, intact: the name Unroofed Sky; Annick and the
  pre-Kiva fragment; what the disk is and that it was released to a bearer; what the
  bearer's book and the ledger say; the reason for the crooked post; any explanation of
  the T-0 entries; any proof about the filter.
