Greta Stroud on the infirmary stair asks what the returned dram was for; **Kiva has an answer ready and does not give it** ("I don't know yet"). Stroud says the night van is a ward short until she can work. Noa at the Pit's mast room (see section 5). | 25, 26 |
| Thu 7th | Compact Library, superseded stacks. **The Reader, Lettice Arkwright, reads the 2446 margin.** Two editions of the *Founders' History*. The Annick boxes and the settlement of 2611. Kiva sees **"O. Dane, teacher, the Crooked Line. Annick, Personal. Read in the room, one afternoon. 2664."** in the open request book and does not ask to be written in. Night: she tells Mara everything; Mara will sign. Noa asks Grader Halloran about the seventh reed. | 25, 26 |
| Fri 8th | Old Yard: Kiva puts the request-book line to Orla. Orla owns having "cut a report to fit," and asks what Kiva has kept from her own team. Noa in the patent room: the Step; Mr. Adlam gives his name. | 26 |
| Sat 9th | Third team **beats the Wren House 8–6** in the New Lane without Kiva. On the bench, Kiva asks Anwen who should stand her corner and withholds her own name (Edda); Anwen says **Pru Finch.** Evening, the Pryce drying yard: the new sheet; Anwen teaches Pru the hem cord; Noa gives his figures aloud and says where he learned of the registers; **Kiva lays the T-0 papers on the sheet and tells Anwen, Kellan, Perrin, Pru and Noa.** Anwen: "I'm not done." She names Perrin to call *off* for Seven. | 26 |
| Sun 10th | **Anwen leaves** on the 5:20 tram and reports to the Graywater hall for Doran Mace's summer line. She asks Kiva to send "the counts," with the *why* column. | 27 |
| Mon 11th | Vane cutaway: the Reader reads his tracing (section 4). Afternoon: **Kiva and Mara are written into the request book.** Fourteen days' notice; the reading is set for Monday the 25th at two. The Reader agrees to seal Mara's page before the box is opened. | 27 |
| Tue 12th | The Circuit's travelling mast is set up in the Old Yard. Vane reads the 2471 entry to Orla, Kiva, Kellan, Perrin and Noa. Orla sets six rules for the tries (section 3). | 27 |
| Wed 13th | Aske passes the knee. **First sanctioned go: called off at the top.** | 27 |
| Thu 14th | An hour in the yard with her back to an empty drum. | 28 |
| Fri 15th | **Second sanctioned go: a crossing, landed on the cross. "One."** | 28 |
| Sat 16th | Third team **loses 6–8 at Stonehand**; Kiva plays one door, no change. Lowen Brask tells her he goes out on a Home Line hold crew on Monday and gives her a caution ("when it comes easy, look at your feet"). | 28 |
| Mon 18th | **Third sanctioned go: a stack.** Called a miss by Noa from the tape. | 28 |
| Tue 19th | Rhea Sorn's card. The sheet is trodden in the Pryces' trough. Kellan says he has been asked to watch a Halden practice. | 28 |
| Wed 20th | **Fourth sanctioned go: a crossing. "Two."** The first two left fingers go white for forty-one counts. Kiva sends Monday's and Wednesday's counts to Anwen. | 28 |
| Thu 21st | Kellan watches the Halden license team at the refinery yard (reported on the 27th). Mace tells Anwen she will take the end of the wall on Tuesday (Anwen cutaway, reported on the 23rd). | 29, 32 |
| Sat 23rd | 5:45 p.m., Graywater hall. 9:40 p.m., false ring, Sag Line mast five; Kiva sees Anwen holding a lamp at the Ferren culvert mouth, sixty paces off; neither signals. | 29 |
| **Sun 24th** | **2:14 a.m., the Weighbridge call.** See the clock below. Closed at 3:40. Kiva sleeps until four in the afternoon. Vane's note arrives. | 29–31 |
| Mon 25th | Orla's weekly book goes to Ysra with the stack in it. 1:52 p.m. Mara's page is sealed. **2 p.m., Kiva reads Annick, Personal.** Evening, Old Yard: no go. | 31 |
| Tue 26th | Aske: **no go until Monday; after that one a week, with the hand seen the next morning.** Grading and three sealed papers are posted (section 7). Noa: no notch on the Weighbridge mast or on either yard crossing. | 32 |
| Wed 27th | Rowan kitchen: **Kellan has said yes to the Halden license team for the Regional** and stays on Team Seven's book. Pru keeps her yellow card. The Even Hand's letter reaches Mara. The sixth company's held tear on the river flats (not shown). | 32 |
| **Thu 28th** | Four o'clock, the Home Line hall. Orla cutaway on the bench. **Kiva reads the bearer's book and writes in it.** | 32 |
| Sat 30th (future) | Perrin takes the team book to Anwen with Mace's runner. | 32 |
| Sun 1st of next month (future) | The watch sits and hears Kiva's entry. The Pit's tape opens to chartered parties. | 31, 32 |
| Mon 2nd (future) | Selka Dray's objection is heard (as stated by Team Twenty-Eight's catcher). | 32 |
| **Tue 3rd, at nine (future)** | All parties before the Placement Chair over the red-tagged bag; "the catcher's book will be wanted." Ysra also wants the tape, the reader and the sponsor that day over the stack. | 32 |
| Tue 26th (as told) | Anwen is to take the end of a wall, "behind nobody." Not shown. | 29 |

Third team's record since the autumn: lost 3–11, lost 7–9, lost 6–7, won 9–8, **won 8–6 (the
9th, without Kiva), lost 6–8 (the 16th).** One meet remains; the page gives it no date.

### The Weighbridge call (Sun 24th) — one pass

Ground: **Weighbridge Street**, running south for a hundred and fifty paces and falling a little
all the way, between two rows of five-story yellow-brick tenements, to a wharf on the canal.
Railings, front steps, and iron gratings in the pavements over the cellars; **a core under
every doorstep and a cell box on every kitchen wall.** Top, west side: the tram sheds (iron
gates, a charging bank that is stepped). East side: the **Hythe Building** nearer the top, and
the **Pellow Building** two-thirds of the way down, with a crack over its door stapled with
iron. The tear: below the Pellow, in the middle of the road. Bottom: **the wharf**, forty paces
wide, no cellars; the weigh-house with the district mast on its roof; the old iron weighbridge
platform set flush in the stone; the canal; the Lantern's lights on the far bank.

| Time | Event |
|---|---|
| 2:14 | The weigh-house bell (three and three). First husk with the bell; it goes in at a ground-floor window halfway down on the west side; the family leaves by the back and pulls its red handle. The street's wardens ring a **dark drill.** |
| 2:24 | Second husk; it sits on the Hythe's step. |
| 2:27 | The night van arrives: Rhea Sorn, Nairn, Pike, Imrie and three more wards, Ruddock, and Team Seven (twelve people). Two residents cannot be moved: an old carter on a breathing bellows at the top of the Hythe, and a child in the Pellow, second floor front (2C), whose physic must be kept cold. |
| about 2:29 | Rhea declares the plan with its reason ("every one of those cellars is a lamp"). Graywater takes the husk on the step (Imrie lights, Pike catches; **sealed at 33**). **Team Seven takes the first husk** on its own sheet on the crown of the road, fourteen paces above the tear, Pru's lure two paces below the south hem: **sealed at 41**, with Nairn standing over them. Perrin carries the bag up the hill. |
| about 2:32 | A third husk steps out two minutes early. Kellan: "Same mark." Nairn has turned to look for Rhea's hand. **Kiva: "Cloth's down. Pru. Lure."** A fourth husk (with a shine) and then **a skinned Flask** come through. The top knot settles on Pru's ward. Rhea shouts for the gate; Pru goes dark; the Flask's leg is already coming down. **Nairn knocks Pru clear and is pinned by the left shin** against a lead weight in the sheet's hem. The gate lights eight paces up the hill and takes the face. The Flask stands over Team Seven and its sheet. The third husk goes to the gate; the fourth to the Pellow's area railings. |
| | **Rhea cuts the fourth husk where it stands**, on the pavement over a cellar grating, with no cloth, stating the trade first ("a window before a cellar… under my name"). The ash goes down the grating. **The Pellow's core trips.** Warden Cobb puts forty-eight flats on their boxes. |
| | **The rescue.** Perrin says he cannot lift and will not claim to. He fetches three shed hands, his father among them, and teaches them a counted *give* and *take.* Three untrained still-doors raise the leg a hand's breadth; Kellan, declared Carry, drags Nairn out; Ruddock boards the leg (both bones). |
| | **Rhea cutaway: roles declared again, aloud, with reasons.** She prints the third husk on her own line's light at the gate ("a Handful of clean tin gone for the sake of a clear gate"). **The turn:** Pru lights at the Pellow's door as a declared lamp; the gate goes dark; the Flask turns and follows her backward down the hill, past the tear on the east side, never nearer than four paces, with Rhea holding her belt and reading the cobbles. Imrie's four re-form twenty paces below and light; "*Mine*"; Pru drops. No join is heard. |
| | A fifth husk comes out. **Rhea gives it to Team Seven and makes them say how first.** Kiva declares: one Handful, plain; cloth on the crown, level with the broken window, above the Pellow; shed hands (Dulcie, Stott) on two corners; Kellan lights a blade at twelve paces, low, for ten counts, walks the husk up onto the cloth and cuts; the Flask is past twenty-five and on a gate. Rhea's one correction: "Who's watching the gap while your heads are down?" Stott is set to face it. **Sealed at 55.** |
| | **The wharf.** Seven sheets in a row, the last doubled on the weighbridge platform. Rhea cuts; Pike takes the odd rungs and Kiva the even. Counts: Pike 36; **Kiva 44**; Pike 37; **Kiva 46** (air off the water; corners up at three); Pike's fifth not stated; **Kiva 52** (her left hand gives out at the second turn; Pru takes the cord on a called three). The skin slides down a rung each time. Rhea's six cuts, by Kellan's watch: 0.7, 0.7, 0.8, 0.9, 1.0, 1.2 seconds. The last two knots are cut together with one level line onto the double sheet: **Pike, sealed at 40.** Kiva, on Pike's north hem, feels the bag "keeping time… loud" and tells him the moment the count closes. |
| 3:40 | **Closed** by the Home Line closing crew (Bettan's call). |
| about 4:00 | Panning on the wharf. Pike's field comb: W, full, on every bag; I, a trace, on every bag; **I, full, on the double bag.** Red tag: *Not for the ground.* |
| 4:40 to 5:00 | Street lamps relit. **Six fouled and quenched boxes at the Pellow's front (ground and first floor) are refilled, and 2C's is topped, from Kiva's second rung**, by the Works night tuner under Warden Cobb's eye. |

Rhea's slate, as read on the wharf: *Tear, 2:14. Closed, 3:40, by the Home Line. Lives: none
lost. Hurt: Nairn, blade, the left leg, both bones. One student bruised. One shed man's
shoulder. Works: the Pellow's core, fouled by the shift officer's cut; a window before a
cellar. Six boxes quenched, and filled again by five. No lamp failed that a person was
depending on. Tinned: 411.8, clean. One Handful printed at the gate; the officer's choice. One
Handful lost down a grating; the officer's choice. 113 put by, stepped, not for the ground.
Remarks: one. An attached team lit a lure inside a string's reading, unasked. The shift
officer had put the question in the van and had not required an answer. The remark is the
shift's.*

Team Seven has therefore stood a whole Flask under a foreman **with** a remark, and **remains
attached.**

## 2. Character state at exit

**Kiva Rowan.** Provisional Third Division, Crooked Line third team. **Ysra's conditions and
Orla's certificate are unchanged** (recovery only, dark hands). At the Weighbridge she opened
no door.
- **The sequence, now also called a crossing.** Four sanctioned goes on the Circuit's
  travelling mast in the Old Yard (section 3). **Two count toward Orla's six.** The Pit's does
  not. She has landed on the cross at the crooked post for the first time.
- What she now knows: that she can hear the pen and must not; that a called *off* in the air
  is a thing Orla will pay for; that she can feel the quarter-dram go "all at once… like a
  breath"; that there is a second, cheaper, softer road in which the still door is opened
  before the Ember has finished shutting, and that it is **a stack**, not a crossing ("the
  gap's the price because the gap's the point… the wall between the rooms"); that the meter
  (the bottom thread) warmed in the air on that go and she did not call it.
- Skills and corrections gained: Pike's "bad-day grip" (the cord at the hooked elbow from the
  first turn; slower by a count); to look the whole circle round before kneeling; **"Who else
  reads it?"** before anything is lit, with range and duration declared; eyes on the gap
  during a count; catching a Flask rung (three, at 44, 46 and 52).
- Her mistakes, in her own book: she answered Noa with the dictionary; she called a lure and
  never asked who else could read it; four of them watched a cord and nobody watched the gap;
  she filed Lowen Brask's caution "on the left" and should have put it on the right; she was
  pleased, the thread said *one,* and she did not.
- **The pattern she is working on** ("not having your answer ready"): withheld with Stroud,
  with Anwen (Edda's name), at the drying yard, with Kellan (three prepared remarks), and on
  the levee with Orla; kept, wrongly, with Noa on the 4th and on the street at 2:32.
- **Injuries.** Left knee: strapped; passed for landings by Aske on the 13th; aches after the
  trough and the wharf. Left first and second fingers: tested on the 6th at their earlier
  state (the first reports late, the second later); **white to the second joint for forty-one
  counts after the fourth go**; the whole left hand "went away" to the wrist for about an hour
  on the wharf and returned by Sunday supper to where it was, slow and late. **Aske on the
  26th: not burned; "it starves, and then it sulks, and each time it sulks a little longer";
  he cannot say how many more times it will come back. No go until Monday; then one a week,
  with the hand seen the next morning.** Permanent loss remains the Movement Five cost. Skinned
  right elbow on the 13th. No fever and no nosebleed in this movement.
- **The disk.** Cold throughout: on the 7th in the stacks; after the second go; in the Library
  on the 25th; on the oak table on the 28th. **It did not warm for the sanctioned crossings.**
  She took it out in front of a stranger on purpose for the first time (Vane). Kennel ash is
  still in three grooves. She asked it nothing.
- **The dream.** No recurrence, asleep or waking. She has now read Hesper Annick's fever
  entry once, twice through, uncopied (section 4). She has a name for it from that page, *the
  sky unroofed,* and no explanation. The name "Unroofed Sky" as a title is not on the page.
- **Papers in her field book now:** the Reader's reading of the 2446 margin, copied; her own
  line and Ilse Corran's line from the bearer's book, copied by right; nothing of Annick's.

**Mara Rowan.** Signed the request book as guardian. **Chose not to read the Annick entry** so
that she can swear she has never seen what her page is held against; her page of the 29th
(pencil, one sheet, the only copy) is sealed under her own wax and the Reader's, hour written
across the join, in the Library's press. She told the sister of the Even Hand to write to her
and not to come at Kiva "through a yard." She has the Order's letter (six questions; she will
answer two). She went to the Old Yard once, about a fortnight before the 28th, and told Orla,
"You kept a thing from my table. Don't do it twice." Her re-sit is still the ninth month. She
does not yet know that the bearer's book records her refusal of 2669 and that the Line sealed
the appointment "regardless"; Kiva is carrying that page home on the 28th.

**Orla Dane.** Read Annick, Personal in the autumn of 2664, during the years when she was not
let sponsor, while looking for any record of training a boy with two doors. Admits she cut her
Sunday report to fit; calls it cowardice "wearing a field officer's coat"; will answer a
straight question or say that she won't; **will not tell Kiva what the box says.** Set the six
rules. **Would have signed the stack**; was stopped by Noa's tape and says so. Reported the
stack in the weekly book; Ysra wants her, Noa and the tape on the 3rd. Waited on the bench on
the 28th and found she did not mind. Ilse Corran took her down her first culvert. What she
will put her name to, when the day comes, is the called *off.*

**Anwen Pryce.** On Mace's summer line from the 10th: a fortnight of nights on the Ferren
culverts, then the Sag; holding a lamp for Works inspectors; told on the 21st that she takes
the end of a wall on the 26th, "behind nobody" (her second week, not her fourth). **Nothing
between her and Kiva is settled, by her own choice:** "I want to go out on that line tomorrow
still minding it, and I want you to let me." She has Kiva's half sheet of the 18th and 20th
and has written nothing back. She was not at the Weighbridge; she heard its bell from the
culvert mouth and was told "Not ours." She does not yet know of Kellan's decision or the
Weighbridge counts.

**Perrin Cade.** Ribs still strapped to the armpits; on a healer's paper (nothing over his
head, no stance under a load). **Calls *off* for Seven and for the yard.** At the Weighbridge
he refused to lift, said so in front of the shift officer, and taught his father and two shed
hands to take a load and give it back. His father: "You never lifted it… Good lad." Went up
the Pellow's stair for the warden. Still for his Hall's second team in the autumn. Will take
the team book to Anwen on the 30th.

**Kellan Renn.** Kept the tally of the two drams to the hundredth. Took an eleven-hundredth
tape for the best number ever seen and was "wrong faster than anybody here." Carry for Nairn.
Lure and blade for the second husk, without a bound. Timed Rhea's cuts. **Has accepted the
Halden license team for the Regional (not the summer, not the yard afterward), in writing; a
house entry is not a transfer and he stays on Team Seven's book.** He thinks Halden's claim on
the bag "lawful" and "wrong," has told Mr. Adlam he will say what he saw to anybody who asks,
and has given a dated copy of that to his mother. He suggests Jude Talley as a blade for Team
Seven at the Regional and says it is not his to fill.

**Noa Bexley.** Knows of the four T-0 entries and the 2446 margin (told by Kiva on the 9th,
after the clerk had told him the volumes had been out). Found the sixteen notches, the Low
Lane's blank tape, and the Step. **Called the stack a miss against a number he wanted.** Keeps
the travelling mast and copies each tape to the Chair. Has stopped calling his page "probably
nothing" and has left its heading bare. He does not know of the dream or what the Annick box
contains.

**Pru Finch.** Seated as Team Seven's reserve Hold under rule fourteen on Rhea Sorn's card (*a
corner and a lamp. Not a wall.*). Lit the lure that turned the Flask; was knocked clear by
Nairn. Then, as a declared lamp, walked a Flask backward down the street for about a hundred
counts and dropped on Imrie's word. Took the cord from Kiva on the sixth rung. **Keeps her
yellow card.** Her reason: to find out "whether I can stay."

**Hollis Vane.** Had the earlier entry in *Directions to the Unplaced* read (section 4) and
amended his own: *Cost: two fingers of the left hand, eased within the hour, returned to their
earlier state and no better.* Obtained the watch's leave for the bearer to read her own line.
Has seen the disk. Witnessed Kiva's entry "who did not prompt." Must read it to the watch on
Sunday and will say that he gave a field direction to that hand and would again. Means to go
to Coper Street about Mara's refusal.

**Rhea Sorn.** Spoke to Kiva for the first time ("Rowan."). Did not ask for the crossing.
Owns the remark as the shift's, the cut at the Pellow, and the printed Handful. **Her one
piece of guidance:** go on saying "I can't weigh it," to everybody, "because the day you stop,
you'll be believed too easily." Told Kiva that Ilse Corran pulled her out of a Ferren culvert
at nineteen with one hand shut on something at her throat: "It didn't do anything. She did."
Says she wrote two words on a card in the spring, has carried it, and will burn it: "They were
the wrong two words." She does not say what they were. Team Seven remains attached to her.

**Others.** **Lowen Brask**: out on a Home Line hold crew from the 18th until the autumn.
**Selka Dray**: asked for, and was given, the reserve blade's seat on Team Twenty-Eight for
the held tear of the 27th; told its catcher she still thinks Kiva should not be seated; her
objection is heard on the 2nd. **Greta Stroud**: could not button her coat on the 6th; "a
spoon now, and not a cup" by the 23rd. **Ysra Crest**: appears only by two notices in her
hand. **Jude Talley**: captained both meets; named by Kellan. **Gower, Edda, Bettan's crew**:
Bettan called the closing; nothing else.

## 3. The six tries

Conditions as written on the 2nd (a sanctioned mast every go; Orla on the crate; Noa reads
after and never during) plus **Orla's six rules of the 12th**, taken from the 2471 entry:

1. A step first, every night, off her own ration; if it is not clean, no go.
2. One go in a night, never two ("not twice in one flood").
3. Before the line she says aloud whether the bottom thread is cold; *one* means she sits.
4. In the air, if the Ember is not shut clean at the top, she calls *off* and comes down as
   herself, on the right leg, rolling.
5. Afterward she shows the left hand first; any white ends the night; white past a slow sixty
   means Aske before the yard.
6. Noa says nothing until she has finished; and one voice besides Orla's may stop it: Perrin.

The sanctioned instrument is the Circuit's travelling mast (one pen, set to her; sealed by the
Placement Chair; Noa copies each tape to the Chair the next morning). **Practice tapes are the
Chair's to show** at a hearing; they are not under the thirty-day rule.

| Go | Date | What happened | Tape (drams) | Counted |
|---|---|---|---:|---|
| 1 | Wed 13th | Listened for the pen; a strut clung; **called *off* at the top**; rolled. Orla: "the first thing you've done in this yard that I'd have paid for." | 0.03 (bound 0.02, a tremble 0.01) | No |
| 2 | Fri 15th | **Crossing.** On the cross; held for eight; given back. | 0.30 (0.02 + 0.25 + 0.03) | **One** |
| 3 | Mon 18th | **A stack**: the still door begun as the Ember ended; no gap; soft; held for eight; thread warm in the air, not called. No cliff on the tape: a round-topped hump, six hundredths over about a third of a second. | 0.11 (0.02 + 0.06 + 0.03) | No |
| 4 | Wed 20th | **Crossing**, chosen with the cheap road in plain sight. Fingers white forty-one counts. | 0.31 (0.02 + 0.26 + 0.03) | **Two** |

**Spent 0.75. In hand 1.25** of the 2.00. Four more crossings at 0.31 are 1.24: **ash for
four and a hundredth, with no miss left in it.** Under Aske's ruling of the 26th the earliest
next go is Monday the 2nd, and then one a week.

Recorded misses now stand at sixteen (the fourteen of the spring, the called *off,* and the
stack); successes at three (the Pit, and two sanctioned).

## 4. The four records

**The 2446 register margin** (Hanley Street; the Works' late record hand, taught to night
clerks until about 2480; read by the Reader on the 7th): *Not for the column, by the Warden's
order. A sister of the Even Hand was at the wicket before it was light. Sent down by her
Master to ask whether the city's lamps had gone in the night. She did not know why she was
sent. Told, yes, all. The Warden says a question is not a fault and is not to be entered.
Entered here. Nobody had told them. R.* The Master of the hill house in 2446 was Hesper
Annick.

**The *Founders' History.*** Ninth edition (given to first-years): *The nine-ringed plate
carried by the first teachers is preserved by the School.* Fourth edition: *The plate of nine
rings went out with the Line and is not the School's to show. It is released to one who
carries it. We have not been told why and have not thought it ours to ask.* The Reader has
asked to see the School's plate for thirty years and is told it is "being conserved."

**Directions to the Unplaced** (Home Line ledger, back section; one entry before Vane's; same
record hand; read on the 11th): *Ferren levee, the spring flood, 2471. Direction to Tansy
Wold, unplaced. Two doors, hand and still. On no school's roll and no company's. One crossing,
hand to still, called aloud before. Reason: the sluice wheel wanted a ward to clear its race
and a stance to turn it, and there was no reaching it twice. Result: turned. Eleven streets
dry. Cost: her left hand, without feeling to the wrist, that night and after. Not returned by
midsummer. She says the crossing took more than both together. I believe her. I would not
direct it twice in one flood, nor to anyone I had not watched miss. E.K., watch.* And beneath:
*Entered on the Line's roll as catcher, autumn. Hand as before.* The Line's roll for the 2470s
was recopied in the 2550s without remarks. Nothing more is known of her.

**Annick, Personal** (the eleventh box; the green notebook; read by Kiva on the 25th). The
page quotes the prologue's entry word for word and in part: *Fever again. Fourth time with
this dream…*; the roads and the different lights; *their light touches mine*; *The sky is not
there*; *Not crushing. Full*; *It is paying attention*; *Every lamp in Meridian answers at
once*; *I wake before I find out. Every time*; *I have told no one in the house*; *The sky
unroofed. That is the only way I can say it.*
- What matches Kiva's account: every road; lights all unlike; the lights touching; no sky;
  what is above coming down; the word *Full*; every lamp answering.
- What differs: Annick is **among** the lights ("mine"); Kiva has only been high up and apart.
  Annick has **no notes and no tick.** Annick gives what the lamps do (out, white, a color
  without a name); Kiva never saw. Annick adds "It is paying attention," which Kiva had not
  had words for and recognizes.
- What it does not give: **a date** (the entry cannot be tied to the lamp night; the margin
  and the notebook "each pointed at the other, and neither one touched it"); a cause; a
  meaning; what the gathering is for; any instruction.
- **Costs paid:** her name and Mara's are in an open book and were sent to the Order and to
  the Halden endowment; both sent an observer; the sister saw that she **counted from the
  third line to the last** and had the Keeper record that the reader's lips moved ("We count
  so in the house, when a thing is asking to be let in"); the endowment had it noted that this
  reader is the subject of a sanctioned tape that opens on the first. All three doors stirred
  while she read and she called the meter over a page for the first time.

**The settlement of 2611**, as the Reader gives it: the box belongs to nobody until the Order
and the endowment agree; any reader may ask for it, in the room, under the Reader's eye, with
no copying; a reader under eighteen needs a guardian's hand; every request is entered in an
open book and sent to both parties the same day, who have fourteen days and may each send one
silent observer. Other lines in the book: the endowment's inspection each spring; a sister who
sits an hour each autumn and does not open it; a scholar thirty years ago; *Halden technical
office, by leave of the endowment; ten boxes, patterns; Personal not asked for,* last spring.

**The bearer's book** (Home Line hall; canvas-bound; a copy of the second book). First leaf, in
print: *One plate, dark, of nine rings, on a cord. Held of the Line. Released to a bearer. /
The bearer is named by the bearer before, and by nobody else. The bearer writes why. The watch
witnesses, and does not choose. / It is not a rank. It is not a reward. We do not know what it
is for. / Write down what it does.* Then: *How it came to the Line is in the first book. The
first book is not here. This is copied from the second, which did not say where the first had
gone.* Added later: *The watch may recall the plate from any bearer, on a vote of the seven,
for cause written* (used once: sold in 2590 for forty drams; recovered in a week).
- The list inside the box lid: seven offices of the watch, never names, "*And the bearer.*"
  Kiva has been on it since 2676 and was never told.
- Five columns: *Bearer. Received, and from whom. Why. What it does. Released, and to whom.*
- The fourth column reads *Nothing* (or *Cold*) for about five hundred years, with one
  *Warm? In a fever. It may have been me.* (margin: *Not entered as fact. Watch.*) and one
  entry in the record hand that nobody present can read; Vane has asked the seven for leave to
  trace it.
- *Corran, I. Received 2643, from M. Gill, at his death.* Gill's why: *She went into a culvert
  after a child and came out counting who was still in it.* Her fourth column: *Nothing.* /
  *Nothing. I talk to it. It's a poor listener.* / *Nothing. I've stopped asking it. I.C.*
- *Released: to K. Rowan, by sealed appointment, 2669. To be delivered on her Kindling day and
  not before. Witnessed by the watch, which did not choose.* **Ilse's why:** *Ione Rowan's
  girl. I sat an hour with her on a stair after the Tallow fire. She is six. She asked me one
  thing the whole time, which was whether anybody had written down what her mother did. I said
  that the Works would. She said, "Will they put why?" / I have looked for a better reason
  than that for a month and have not found one. I do not know that it wants one. I.C.*
- A clerk's line: *Guardian (M. Rowan, aunt) was asked, and said no. Appointment sealed
  regardless; it is the bearer's right. The Line owes that household an answer and has not got
  one. Watch.*
- *Rowan, K. Received 2676, by courier, sealed, from I. Corran (d. 2673).* **Kiva's entry in
  the fourth column, the 28th:** *Warm, once. Wednesday the 29th of last month, at the Pit, a
  little before half past ten in the forenoon. First felt after I had shut the still door, at
  the end of a crossing, Ember to still. I do not know when it began. It lasted from the south
  shelf to the top of the road stair. Cold by the keeper's gate. / Cold since. Cold through two
  crossings more, on a sanctioned mast, the 15th and the 20th. / I did not ask it anything. It
  did not do anything. K.R.* Vane: *Entered in the bearer's hand, in the presence of the watch
  officer, Sag Line, who did not prompt. H.V.*
- The bearer may copy her own line and the line that gave it to her, and show them where she
  likes; not another's.

## 5. Instrument and ash evidence

- **The Pit tape, 29th.** Every one of the sixteen pens shows a hair-high notch at one
  instant, **eleven or twelve seconds after Kiva's cliff** (Noa, four measurements). The
  keeper's "about forty seconds" was a guess made while running the rim. The Low Lane mast,
  two miles off, shows nothing at that minute: **it was the Pit and stopped at the Pit's
  wall.** Noa cannot say whether sixteen people jumped or the mast did.
- **No notch** on the weigh-house mast for the whole of the Weighbridge call, and none after
  either sanctioned crossing (one pen; no ring of lamps). "It isn't every
  crossing. Or it's only the first. Or it wants more than one pen to show." Noa will not
  choose.
- **The seventh reed** is "cut to the Step" (Halloran). **Halden's foundation patent, *A
  Method of Stepping Cells in Bank,* 2391:** a row of cells is salted late with one pinch of
  ash already made to keep a beat, and thereafter beats as one and can be driven by one
  operator. Used in Halden's yards, refineries, lift yards and the big tram sheds. **Pump
  houses and tenement cell boxes are not stepped**; each cell keeps its own time. Named before
  the patent *counting ash* (Wren House cards 40 to 44, superseded). The claim: *The Proprietor
  claims the Step, and all ash exhibiting the Step, however produced.* The caution: *Not to be
  introduced to an unstepped bank under load.*
- **This spring's ash.** Every bag at the Weighbridge read W, full; I, a trace (as at the
  Pit). **The last two knots of the Flask, the pair that carry the legs, read I, full:** the
  reed "knocked… like a knuckle on a door"; Kiva felt "a shed full" of pens; a shed foreman of
  thirty years knew it by hand as "shed ash" and would pay a week's wages for it and "not put a
  thimble of it in my own kitchen." Pike, twenty-six years a catcher, had never heard that
  reed over a rift's leavings before this spring.
- Nobody on the page says why stepped ash is coming out of a tear. Noa's thought (something
  "listening to the banks of Meridian") is marked by him as a guess and not written down.

## 6. Team Seven's ash ledger

| Date | Entry | Sealed medical (W) | Practice glass | Held | Credited, unentered | Notes |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|
| Sun 3rd (entry) | Carried from Movement Three | 2.0 | 2.00 | 1.6 | 0 | 1.0 returned to the Wren House infirmary at par (Stroud has been shown the line). Sheet and poles bought (3.0). |
| Wed 13th | Go 1 | 2.0 | 1.97 | 1.6 | 0 | 0.03 by the mast. |
| Fri 15th | Go 2 | 2.0 | 1.67 | 1.6 | 0 | 0.30. |
| Mon 18th | Go 3 | 2.0 | 1.56 | 1.6 | 0 | 0.11. |
| Wed 20th | Go 4 | 2.0 | 1.25 | 1.6 | 0 | 0.31. |
| Tue 26th | **Weighbridge share graded and posted** | 2.0 | 1.25 | 1.6 | **8.4** | Yield 44.8 (22.6 + 22.2), Grade First, W full, I trace. Civic half 22.4, paid on the ground; casualty reserve 5.6; schools 8.4; team 8.4. |

- **The 8.4 cannot be entered until four have signed.** A team book may go to a member on
  service by a member's hand; Perrin takes it to Anwen on the 30th. No use for it has been
  proposed on the page.
- The sealed W reserve is untouched. The 1.6 is untouched.
- Pru Finch is paid off the Circuit's reserve card, not the team book.
- The steps before each go came off Kiva's own weekly ration, not the glass.
- Individual metered ash: each of the four settled a field dram on the company's meter at the
  Graywater hall on the 23rd; spends are not stated.
- The purse sheet has a mark "the size of a dinner plate" where a Flask's leg stood and a
  blood stain on the south hem. It was used for both of Team Seven's husks.

**Standing:** *T7. Total yield, 116.8. Sixth of thirty-one. (att.)* (72.0 + 44.8.)

**The call's yield:** 411.8 drams clean (the page gives the total, Team Seven's two bags and
no other single figure); 113 stepped and withheld; one Handful printed and salted; one Handful
lost down a grating.

## 7. Institutional state

- **Three sealed papers on the Measure Hall board (26th):** the Halden Works claims the
  113-dram bag under the foundation patent and "asks on what paper" it was kept from civic use
  "on the word of a provisional candidate, unclassified, and a field comb"; the wardens of
  Weighbridge Street claim it by ground-first and ask only "that it not be carried off the
  street's account by anybody who was not there"; Wren House holds it for reference (*I, full,
  from a field source: without precedent on the cards. Halloran*). **All parties before the
  Placement Chair, Tuesday the 3rd, at nine. "The catcher's book will be wanted."**
- **The district table** in the Graywater watch room, before the call: Saltmere first (27
  calls, 27 closed, two hurt, no hours dark, 2,840 drams); Graywater second (31, all closed,
  five hurt, six hours dark on one branch, 3,910, one remark); Halden's First Company third
  (4,400, one call held over, two remarks). Columns: *Closed. Lives. Works. Tinned. Remarks.*
  "You read it as you'd read a line of print… You can't buy a column with the one to its
  right." By Rhea's slate Graywater takes a second remark; the table is not shown again.
- **Rules cited:** fourteen (an attached team may seat a first-step reserve in one declared
  role, at the chartered officer's pleasure); nineteen (a house entry for the Regional is not
  a transfer). **Ground first:** half of what is clean off a call goes into that street the
  same night.
- **The Pellow Building:** forty-eight flats; its core fouled "to the lid," not to be opened
  by the Works before Tuesday the 26th; on its boxes. Its cell book is the eleventh volume and
  carries on its first leaf, copied forward, the installer's line of 2247 (*Why: child's
  medicine. Cells come back clean or they don't come in.*); **the installer's name was dropped
  by whoever copied the third volume.** Warden Cobb's entry of the 24th names the ash by its
  tag, "*Catcher: K. Rowan,*" on "the catcher's word, and the comb's, and Warden Cobb's."
- **The Order of the Even Hand** asked leave to write to Kiva's sponsor; Mara redirected it to
  herself. **The Halden endowment** asked the Library for nothing. **The Home Line watch** (the
  seven) sits on Sunday the 1st and will hear the first entry in five hundred years that is
  not *nothing* or a doubt.
- **The Circuit's tape of the Pit** opens to chartered parties on the 1st; Mr. Adlam has said
  twice that he will read it then.

## 8. New people and places

People (pronouns as written; author's choice):
- **Lettice Arkwright**, "Reader" (she; small, upright, two pairs of spectacles; has
  administered the 2611 settlement thirty-one years; reads the late record hand).
- The **Hanley Street clerk** (he; thin, careful, a cough; still unnamed). **R.**, a Works
  night clerk of 2446. **E.K.**, watch, 2471. **Tansy Wold** (she; 2471).
- **Mr. Adlam** (he; about forty; the Halden technical office; the pleasant man of chapter 24,
  now named; also the endowment's observer).
- A **sister of the Even Hand** (she; about sixty; grey; cropped hair; had not spoken in the
  Reader's hearing in twenty years; unnamed).
- Graywater: **Pike** (he; the square catcher of chapter 18, now named; twenty-six years; a
  tendon cut at thirty); **Nairn** (he; second blade; about twenty-three; left leg, both bones,
  until the autumn); **Ruddock** (he; bald; drives the van and is the crew's healer); Imrie and
  three more wards; Doran Mace seen on the hall steps and heard on the line. **Ostrey** is a
  name in Nairn's mouth only.
- Weighbridge: **Perrin's father** (still unnamed on the page; short, wide, grey mustache; a
  still-door who never went to a Hall; thirty years on the sheds' charging bank; works
  nights); **Dulcie** (she; tall) and **Stott** (he; young, thick-necked), shed hands;
  **Warden Cobb** of the Pellow (she; about seventy; a man's overcoat, a handbell, the ledger
  chained to her wrist); a lamp warden (he); the mother and child in 2C and the carter at the
  Hythe (unseen); a family from the west-side ground floor.
- Works: **Brannock** (he; the night tuner; he was a name only until now).
- **Pettit**, the Halden license team's Kill (a name only). **M. Gill**, bearer before Corran
  (he; a name in the book).
- Mrs. Pryce appears; Team Twenty-Eight's catcher again (still unnamed).

Places: the Compact Library's **superseded stacks** (three flights of iron stairs below the
ground floor) and the small room off the reading room; the **patent room** on the first floor
of the Measure Hall annex; the Graywater **watch room** (map wall with a lamp for every mast
and a bell; the painted district table) and hall yard; **Weighbridge Street** and its wharf
(above); the **Home Line hall** (a whitewashed passage with a bench; a small room with an oak
table and a window over the Ferren levee); the Pryce laundry's **drying yard** and stone
trough; the refinery yard where the Halden team trains (off the page). Bell codes: two and two
for Sag Line mast five; three and three for the weigh-house.

All new names were searched for in this book's manuscript and provenance, the other books'
manuscripts and registries, and `universe/` before use; none matched. "Lowmarket" was
considered for the district and rejected: the prologue places it ten days downriver.

## 9. Plants and open threads carried forward

| Plant | Status at exit |
|---|---|
| Six witnessed repeats | **Two of six.** 1.25 drams in hand: four at 0.31 and a hundredth. One a week from Monday the 2nd, on Aske's price. |
| The crossing's cost | Still a cliff of about a quarter-dram with nothing open. Unexplained. A stack costs a third as much and is forbidden. |
| Two-finger sensation | Longer each time: forty-one counts white on the 20th; the hand gone for an hour on the 24th. Not burned. **Permanent loss reserved for Movement Five.** Tansy Wold's hand is the precedent on record. |
| Disk | Cold. Its one warmth is now entered in the bearer's book in Kiva's hand. Held of the Line; recallable on a vote of seven for cause written. |
| What the disk is for | *We do not know what it is for.* The first book is lost. |
| One unread fourth-column entry in the record hand | Vane has asked the seven for leave to trace it. |

| 2604 entry | Untouched. Nobody has asked who was ill that night. |

| Why a rift is throwing stepped ash | Not said. |
| Orla and Kiva | Level, not mended. Each has read the box and will not tell the other what it says. |
| Orla's standing | The stack is on a sanctioned tape and in the weekly book; she is called for the 3rd. She would have signed it. |
| Anwen and Kiva | Unsettled by Anwen's choice. Counts go out by the runner; nothing has come back. |
| Team Seven | Kellan to a Halden license team for the Regional, still on the book. Perrin to his Hall in the autumn. Anwen away six weeks from the 10th. Pru a standing reserve. No blade named for the Regional; Jude Talley suggested. |

| Mara and the Line | The 2669 refusal and the Line's "regardless" are in a book that Kiva has now copied. Vane means to call. |
| Rhea's card | To be burned; its two words never said to Kiva. |
| The Pellow's core | Fouled; the Works after the 26th. 2C's cupboard on a topped box. |
| Mara's re-sit; the crooked post's reason; Jessamy Hart; the Sag baselines; the referee's note; Greta Stroud's verdict on the dark floor | Untouched or still open. Stroud has "half liked" one thing Kiva said. |

## 10. Items for owner or editorial attention

Routes the author chose where the packet left room, and places where the draft may have gone
further than intended:

1. **The district is Weighbridge Street and the Pellow Building** of prologue snapshot 8. The
   building's cell book carries the 2247 installer's reason on its first leaf and has lost his
   name. This is the largest reuse of prologue material so far.
2. **The students' failure is a lure.** The first-step method (a small lit ward to draw a
   husk) is read by a Flask. The three habits the packet names are spread across three people:
   Kellan's count ("under forty"), Kiva's reused mark, and nobody watching the tear. Kiva gives
   the word. A professional (Nairn) is hurt by it, and the shift officer takes the remark as
   hers for not having required an answer in the van.
3. **Rhea Sorn deliberately fouls a tenement core** to keep a husk out of an occupied flat,
   and prints a Handful to clear her gate. Both are stated as trades before she acts.
4. **Kiva opens no door at the Weighbridge.** Her contribution is three rung catches, the
   corrected husk, and telling Pike that a bag keeps time. The decision to withhold the bag is
   Pike's, on a comb; the choice of ash for the Pellow is Warden Cobb's.
5. **"Stepped" ash.** The seventh reed (I) is now tied to a Halden foundation patent with the
   clause "however produced." Municipal banks and tenement boxes are **not** stepped (this
   keeps faith with the pump bank going "into step" as an abnormal event in chapter 24). The
   bottom two knots read I, full. This is the movement's largest invention and it creates the
   Movement Five ownership dispute; it also narrows what the spring's oddity can later be.
6. **"A crossing"** is given as the Home Line's older word for a sequence, from a 2471 entry
   about a two-door woman, Tansy Wold, whose left hand did not return. She is not a bearer.
   The owner may want a different precedent, or none.
7. **A third kind of go, the stack,** which lands softly, costs a third, and would have been
   signed by Orla. It is the "different failure" the packet asks for. It also establishes that
   a correct crossing is expensive on purpose.
8. **Aske's new ruling (one go a week)** and the exact ash (1.25) leave Movement Five no room
   for a miss.
9. **Kellan accepts the Halden team.** The packet says he is weighing it and that supporting
   characters may decide. He remains on Team Seven's book and may stand the owed Flask.
10. **Rhea tells Kiva about Ilse Corran and the culvert** and says she will burn a card. The
    words "Corran's disk" are still not said. This spends part of a Movement Two plant.
11. **The bearer's book** is drafted in full: a rule leaf, a recall clause, five columns, about
    five hundred years of *Nothing,* one doubted *Warm?*, one unread record-hand entry, M. Gill
    as the bearer before Corran, and Ilse's reason. **Kiva writes in the fourth column.** If the
    owner wants the disk's earlier behavior left wholly blank, the doubted entry and the unread
    one are the lines to cut.
12. **Ilse's reason** ("Will they put why?") is the author's. It is deliberately small.
13. **Mara's 2669 refusal is on the Line's record** as overridden. Prologue snapshot 11
    has Mara say she told Ilse no; the book now shows the Line went round her.
14. **The sister of the Even Hand speaks** after twenty years' silence in that room, and says
    the Order "did not burn it." The 2603 prologue says the Order wanted the papers back to
    burn some. Both can be true; review should check the tone.
15. **Kiva counts through the reading** and all three doors stir. Nothing is lit and no cell is
    near. An observer notices. If the owner wants the reading to leave no trace, this is the
    beat to change.
16. **Mr. Adlam** is one man in three rooms (mast room, patent room, the Library). He is kept
    pleasant and lawful.
17. **The Pit's interval is now eleven or twelve seconds**, which agrees with the recheck's
    note that the keeper's forty was an estimate. The notch on all sixteen pens is new
    evidence and is left unexplained.
18. **Institutional inventions of this draft:** the settlement of 2611; the Library's request
    book; *Directions to the Unplaced*; the travelling mast; rules fourteen and nineteen; the
    district table and its order of columns; ground first; the dark drill; a field comb; the
    watch of seven and its Sunday sitting; that a team book may travel to a member on service.
19. **Standing "sixth of thirty-one"** after the Weighbridge is the author's figure; no other
    team's yield is given.
20. **Movement length is 45,124 words** (`wc -w`), at the foot of the 45,000–55,000 working
    range. Chapters run 5,007 to 6,469. Forty-one sections, about 1,100 words a section.
    **Secondary viewpoint is about 12.9%** by plain section word count (Noa 1,618; Rhea 1,650;
    Vane 1,021; Orla 766; Anwen 756); Kiva holds about 87.1%.
21. **No formula target has been measured** for these chapters. Narration was written longer
    than in earlier first drafts on purpose; that is an intention, not a result.
22. Small facts taken from the checkpoints and not re-read in the manuscript should be checked:
    Brannock's Tallis Street tunes; Perrin's father and the tram yards; Rhea's poster on Anwen's
    door; the Kennel stair where Kiva stopped asking the disk; that Orla's sponsoring standing
    (not her teaching) was what was suspended; the year of the Regional trial (given here as
    2661).
23. Elapsed-time phrases worth a mechanical check: "seven weeks" (the 13th to the 4th); "two
    hundred and thirty-four years"; "sixteen years" (2664); "two hundred and nine years"
    (2471); "four hundred and thirty-three years" (2247); "eleven days" (Vane's tracing);
    "twenty-six days" (Noa, on the 4th); "three weeks ago" (Pru's wall); "a fortnight ago"
    (Mara in the yard); "eleven years" (2669).
24. Pronouns chosen by the author: Pike, Nairn, Ruddock, Adlam, Stott, Brannock, Gill, the
    clerk and the lamp warden he; Arkwright, Wold, Dulcie, Cobb and the sister she.

## 11. Deliberately not done

The disk does not warm, teach, speak, map, answer, or act; it is cold through two sanctioned
crossings and through the call. No tactic comes from the disk or the dream. No fever and no
recurrence. The Unroofed Sky is not named as such; Annick's own phrase appears once in
quotation and once in Kiva's thought. The cause of the Fall, the filter theory, the disk's
origin and the Makers are not explained; the bearer's book says in print that the Line does
not know what the plate is for. No door of Kiva's is opened in the field. No two doors are
held open on purpose and no three; the one stack is called a miss. She does not go from one to
six: two of six. No rank changes; no hold or bench figure is re-measured. The fingers are
neither cured nor lost. The sealed W reserve is not touched. No new spawn expression is
introduced. Anwen is not on Team Seven after the 10th and is not at the call. Lowen Brask is
not made a villain and Selka Dray is not made a friend. Nobody connects the stepped ash, the
T-0 entries, the notch, the disk and the dream; Noa refuses to choose among five explanations.
Nothing in this movement has been reviewed, scored or repaired, and Movement Five has not been
started. The author made no commit or push; another process committed and pushed the chapter
files as they were written (see `AUTHORSHIP.md`), and its copies of chapters 29 and 32 predate
the author's last edits to those two files.

## 12. What Movement Five opens on

State on the evening of Thursday the 28th:

- **Ownership.** The disk is "held of the Line," released to a bearer, and recallable; the
  School's ninth edition says the School has it; a watch of seven hears on Sunday that it has
  been warm. A 113-dram bag is claimed by a patent, by a street, and by a reference shelf.
- **Proof.** A sealed, timed page in the Library's press; a request book with two signatures;
  an uncopied notebook entry with no date; a tape with a cliff and sixteen notches; two clean
  crossings and one stack on a sanctioned mast; a catcher's tag in a tenement's ledger; an
  entry in the bearer's book witnessed by a watch officer "who did not prompt."
- **Sponsorship.** Orla is called before the Chair with her reader and a tape that shows she
  could not tell a crossing from a stack with a stick. The Order has asked for the sponsor and
  been given the guardian. The Line's own record says it overrode that guardian in 2669.
- **The Regional Measure.** Team Seven is sixth by yield, attached, one blade pledged to a
  Halden team, its Hold on a Graywater line until late next month, its Carry strapped, and a
  first-step reserve with a yellow card.
- **The six.** Two, and four to show, one a week at soonest, with ash for four and a
  hundredth, and a hand that stays away longer each time.
- Withheld for Movement Five and after, intact: the three-door transition; the permanent cost
  to the two fingers; what the stepped ash means; what the watch, the Chair, the Order and
  Halden each decide; the unread entry; the 2604 dreamer, if there was one; the reason for the
  crooked post; any proof about the filter.

## PROJECT EVIDENCE — books/book-2680-opening/STATE_LEDGER.md

# Kindling 2680 opening state ledger

Status: opening locks resolved 2026-10-08. Ready for the prologue and first
eight-chapter continuous prose run.

## Prologue and opening day

- Year 2680, during the six-hundredth-anniversary season of the Fall.
- The prologue's final historical snapshot is 2676: **Kiva Rowan**, thirteen,
  reaches the Kindling uninjured and opens all three known doors.
- Chapter One begins four years later. Kiva is seventeen and seeking collegiate
  placement for the 2680 season.
- Kiva lives with her aunt and legal guardian, **Mara Rowan**, in Meridian. Mara
  repairs municipal instruments and reconciles their paper records.
- The Satori disk reached Kiva through a sealed Home Line appointment by its prior
  bearer, not through blood descent. Kiva knows it is old and entrusted; she does
  not know its full public history or origin.
- Meridian's schools expect one first door and have procedures for unusual strength,
  but no accepted process for three simultaneous traditions.

## Knowledge boundaries

- General public: knows the Fall, Kindling, ranks, schools, Riftspawn, and ordinary
  filter language as history/science appropriate to the final world-state choice.
- Students: know institutional versions of Home Line history, not necessarily the
  surviving founder records.
- Orla Dane: knows the disk family and at least one suppressed prior anomaly.
- Kiva: does not know the disk's true origin, the full filter theory, or why
  all three doors opened.
- Kiva remembers the 2676 fever dream in sensory fragments: Glory-level figures
  converging, the sky/filter absent, pure presence pressing into the world, and every
  ash-lit thing responding. She does not know the later name **Unroofed Sky**, whether
  the dream predicts anything, or that older Glory accounts overlap it.
- Orla knows there are disputed historical fever accounts but does not begin Book One
  with proof that Kiva's dream matches them.
- Reader: sees evidence before explanation; no prologue solves the mystery.

## Physical and progression state

- All three doors open at Kindling, but none is mastered.
- The event leaves three fine, asymmetrical heat-lines from Kiva's sternum toward
  her left shoulder. They remain visible and become tender when the doors compete.
- The disk warms once, only after Kiva completes the doors in a correct deliberate
  sequence. It never instructs her or grants technique.
- The disk can amplify or carry the Unroofed Sky fever dream, but it cannot trigger
  on demand, answer questions, or supply a combat/engineering solution.
- Initial classification is incomplete or contradictory, not a secret top rank.

## Social state

- Kiva has a real home/family obligation in Mara's repair-and-records work.
- Anwen Pryce, Perrin Cade, and Kellan Renn have independent placements and
  goals before meeting the protagonist.
- Institutions have defensible reasons to fear a category they cannot safely test.

## Institutional and world locks

- Campus compact: **the Meridian Compact**.
- Mixed-path college: **the Crooked Line School**.
- Resilience/body college: **Stonehand Hall**.
- Medical, Ward, Sustain, and ash-engineering college: **Wren House**.
- Collegiate competition: **the Meridian Collegiate Circuit**; final regional
  event: **the Regional Measure**. Events include individual Measures, team lanes,
  ashcraft trials, rescue/extraction problems, and controlled Riftspawn engagements.
- The collegiate curriculum includes a staged field license: contained spawn anatomy
  and kill drills, supervised Handful kills, live-rift recovery, then higher-yield team
  responses. Book One devotes several connected chapters to Kiva's team learning how
  to kill Riftspawn and recover the ash cleanly before the first major live response.
- Above college are **chartered rift companies**: salaried professional teams that
  fight Riftspawn, close or contain rifts, rescue civilians, protect infrastructure,
  and recover ash. Standings reward closure, lives saved, protected infrastructure,
  clean ash, and rule compliance—not kills alone.
- Book One is regional. Book Two expands toward national competition and company
  politics. Book Three becomes international as the filter and ash grid destabilize.
- Mature hardened regional technology and scheduled wormhole stations exist. Travel
  is expensive and bounded; interplanetary normality does not.
- Classification uses physical instruments, projected tables, and human rulings.
  There is no private retinal or software-like status overlay.
- **Draw** is ash spent on standard work divided by that task's book/bench expectation:
  `1.0` is standard and lower is more efficient. A contested four-minute hold uses a
  book expectation of twelve hundredths of a dram.

## Ash-electric civilization and Glory

- Refined ash is the dominant finite energy source. **Ash induction** converts a
  controlled ash reaction into ordinary current for lights, pumps, heat,
  communications, medicine, workshops, transit, arenas, and rift sensing.
- Ash does not create free energy. Cells are graded, metered, consumed, reconciled,
  and vulnerable to contamination or runaway.
- **Ashcraft** blends magic and ash: a trained soul supplies pattern, intention,
  and control; ownerless ash supplies most of the fuel. Different traditions shape,
  contain, conduct, or sustain the reaction differently.
- Modern cells continue running after a Kindled operator starts and tunes them, but
  still require maintenance, replacement ash, and emergency shutdown.
- At **Glory**, a rare master can impress stable patterns at civic or industrial
  scale. Glory does not make ash infinite; it expands safe throughput, duration,
  precision, and the complexity of blended systems.
- That capability produces ash-power industrial dynasties as well as school orders
  whose masters pursue near-monastic devotion to one tradition. Some orders reject
  corporate ownership; some industry houses sponsor them; some Glory masters move
  between both worlds.
- Pro rift companies feed the ash grid. This creates prestige and public service,
  but also incentives to overhunt, hide risk, manipulate ash grades, or protect
  wealthy districts first.
- A sanctioned kill produces a recorded yield. Civic power, casualty reserve, school,
  and team shares are removed in that order. Student shares fund training cells,
  metered combat techniques, equipment, travel, and medical reserve; they are not
  unmetered personal treasure.
- Ash is recorded by **yield** (Handful, Flask, Barrel, Well), **grade** (purity and
  stability), and **resonance** (which patterns can use that spawn expression safely).
  A large dirty yield may be worth less than a small stable one.
- Killing does not grant rank automatically. Real combat can deepen capacity because
  the practitioner works at the edge of owned control and must later reproduce the
  lesson. Ash funds the next threshold; training makes the threshold survivable.
- Filter thinning increases both human potential and Riftspawn pressure. The opening
  book presents this first as unusually capable students and an unusually varied rift
  season. Later books prove the common Glory-pressure cause.

## First-season lasting cost

During the high-stakes regional sequence Kiva will permanently lose fine sensation
in the first two fingers of her left hand. Healing closes the injury but does not
restore exact touch. The cost is planted before it occurs and changes how she grips,
trains, and asks for help. Rank cannot erase it.

## Movement Three carried state (chapters 17–24)

Recorded 2026-10-11 after the consolidated same-author repair. **Passed by a separate
same-model informed targeted recheck; no second prose repair is justified.** The manuscript governs; full detail is in
`provenance/MOVEMENT-003-CONTINUITY.md`.

- **Calendar.** The month is still unnamed and has thirty days. The fifth company's Flask
  qualifier at the Pit is **Wednesday the 29th** (the first draft's "26th" was wrong by
  its own weekdays). Grading Thursday the 30th; board findings and standings **Friday the
  1st**; the investment vote **Saturday the 2nd**; the Old Yard **Sunday the 3rd**.
  Movement Four opens after Sunday the 3rd.
- **The sequence.** Kiva has completed one correct deliberate two-door sequence, Stride
  into stance: about nine seconds of stance, 0.31 of a dram (0.02 + 0.26 + 0.03), Draw
  near twelve. It was done under a recorded Home Line field direction, after the event
  was struck, outside her certificate. It is not certified. Orla's standard is "Show me
  six"; Team Seven has funded six sanctioned tries on three written conditions (a
  sanctioned mast every go; Orla on the crate; Noa reads after and never during).
- **The disk** warmed once, on the 29th. Kiva **first felt it after** she had shut the
  still door; nobody knows when it began. It was cold by the keeper's gate and has been

- **Body.** Stood down until the **13th**: no lanes, no floors, nothing lit, no landings

- **Team Seven.** Ninth of thirty-one by yield (72.0), **second step, attached**, to the

- **Unsettled between people.** Anwen and Kiva (the gate, asked twice); Gower of Old Four;

- **Institutional.** The Circuit's book now reads "A gate shall be left lit" (from the

  chartered party on the **1st of the month after** (thirty days from Friday the 1st).
- **Knowledge.** Kiva holds four T-0 entries (2446, 2604, 2676, and the Pit's lamp ring,
  10:24 and about forty seconds) and the unread 2446 margin; she has kept them from the
  team and means to ask the Compact Library's reader herself. Noa knows of the lamps from
  the keeper and does not know what a T-0 is. Orla has named a bearer's book at the Home
  Line hall and asked Hollis Vane for it; Vane found an earlier hand in the field-direction

- **Lane calls.** "Half!" names the side that earned it; "Down!" names the side that lost

## Movement Four carried state (chapters 25–32)

Recorded 2026-10-11 from the first draft, by its author, directly after the run.
**Unreviewed: no editorial review, cold read, repair or recheck has been run.** The
manuscript governs; full detail is in `provenance/MOVEMENT-004-CONTINUITY.md`. Only what
the prose shows is entered here.

- **Calendar.** The month is still unnamed and has thirty days. Movement Four runs from
  Monday the 4th to Thursday the 28th. Anwen left on Sunday the 10th. The Weighbridge call
  was Sunday the 24th (bell 2:14 a.m.; closed 3:40). The Annick reading was Monday the 25th
  at two. The Home Line hall was Thursday the 28th. Ahead: Saturday the 30th (Perrin takes
  the team book to Anwen); **Sunday the 1st** (the Home Line watch sits; the Pit's tape
  opens); Monday the 2nd (Selka Dray's objection); **Tuesday the 3rd at nine** (all parties
  before the Placement Chair; the catcher's book, the tape, Noa and Orla are wanted).
- **The sequence ("a crossing").** Four sanctioned goes on the Circuit's travelling mast in
  the Old Yard: a called *off* (0.03), a crossing (0.30), a **stack** (0.11, called a miss by

  in hand, which is four crossings and a hundredth.** Orla's six rules of the 12th stand (a
  step first; one go a night; the thread called cold aloud; *off* in the air if the Ember is
  not shut clean; the left hand shown first; Noa silent until after, and Perrin may stop it).
  A crossing costs about a quarter-dram in the gap with nothing open; a stack (the second
  door begun before the first is shut) costs a third as much, lands softly, and is forbidden.
- **The disk** stayed cold through both sanctioned crossings, the call, the reading and the

- **The dream.** No recurrence. Kiva has read Hesper Annick's fever entry (undated; the green

  roads, the unlike lights, the touching, the absent sky, the word "Full," and every lamp

- **Certificate and conditions.** Unchanged. Kiva opened no door in the field. The stack is

- **Team Seven.** **Sixth of thirty-one by yield (116.8), still second step, attached**, to

- **Instrument evidence.** The Pit's tape shows a notch on all sixteen pens eleven or twelve

- **Words.** "A crossing" is the Home Line's older word for a sequence (ledger entry of 2471,

  and "the Step" are Halden's; "counting ash" was the Wren House's earlier name.
- **Names and pronouns introduced (author's choice, pending owner review).** Lettice

## PROJECT EVIDENCE — books/book-2680-opening/BOOK_MAP.md

# Kindling 2680 — Book One map

Status: drafting map. Opening locks resolved 2026-10-08; prose run authorized.

## 1. Book promise

Kiva Rowan opened all three known doors at thirteen and spent four years classified
below what any one of them might have earned alone. In 2680 she enters Meridian's
collegiate competition system, the regional feeder for professional rift companies.
Across one season, she must turn breadth into reliable control, learn through school
combat, train through several connected chapters to kill Riftspawn and capture their
ash, keep friendships strained by ranking, yield shares, and recruitment, and answer
a real rift event where category matters less than whether the old Home Line method
still works. Beneath that season, Kiva's recurring Unroofed Sky fever dream shows
Glory users converging as the planetary filter disappears.

## 2. Ending promise

Kiva completes the collegiate season, earns a public place without receiving a
finished explanation, and deliberately combines the doors once under rules the
reader understands. The win costs something that cannot be reset by rank. The disk
is recognized as belonging to the old bearer line; a professional company makes a
bounded recruitment offer; and evidence from the emergency shows the filter trend is
accelerating. A second historical account confirms that part of Kiva's fever dream
predates her, without explaining it. Book One closes regionally. Book Two's national
competition, energy-industry conflict, and shared-prophecy problem are opened, not
substituted for the ending.

## 3. Numerical and structural shape

- One planned historical prologue precedes Chapter 1. It must establish the EMP,
  fallout, failed restoration, first Kindlings, ash's gradual industrial adoption,
  radiation-safe nuclear recovery, ash-nuclear synthesis, distributed reactors,
  flight, Glory industry, devoted school orders, and rebuilding through ten human
  scenes without answering the reserved cause of the Fall. Its final snapshot shows
  Kiva's anomalous Kindling and first Unroofed Sky fever dream at thirteen in 2676.
  Chapter One jumps four years to the 2680 collegiate season.
- Six movements, eight chapters each; 48 chapters working total.
- Working length 270,000–315,000 words, adjusted for actual scene need.
- Approximately 87% protagonist viewpoint/focus and 13% purposeful secondary
  focus across four or five characters.
- O'Connor numerical prose targets apply: 14.6-word mean, 11-word median, about
  27.7% sentences of five words or fewer, about 3.3% of forty words or more,
  short paragraphs, accessible approximately seventh-grade readability, and
  front-loaded progression teaching.
- Major fights receive developed tactical space. No required fight count replaces
  story need, but every movement has a physical test, field exercise, or deliberate
  quiet reason.

## 4. Six movements

| Movement | Entry pressure | Learning / relationship engine | Action and turn | Exit state |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **1 — Three Doors** (Ch. 1–8) | Four years after her anomalous Kindling and fever dream, Kiva enters collegiate placement with partial training but no safe combined classification | Establish teams, standings, scholarships, pro recruitment, Mara's ash-nuclear repair work, the three traditions, and rank versus control | A placement trial combining combat, ashcraft, and rescue fails in an original way; Kiva protects someone but worsens her mark; the dream recurs as a brief physical interruption rather than guidance | Provisional lowest-division roster place; Orla takes accountable sponsorship; a pro scout notices the disk only after Kiva earns attention |
| **2 — The Crooked Lane** (Ch. 9–16) | Kiva trains inside a school system built for one-door specialists and is treated as both weak and dangerous | Several connected chapters teach spawn anatomy, kill roles, declared aborts, containment, dissolution capture, ash grading, and the team's first earned yield; rivalry, humor, and team identity develop under real fear | A supervised Handful kill and recovery lead into the first sanctioned collegiate meet: human opponents exploit habits the spawn drill created. A useful loss proves control matters more than access | Kiva earns continued roster standing; Team Seven owns its first small ash share; Noa records output, recovery efficiency, and door behavior that do not fit the institutional model |
| **3 — First Season** (Ch. 17–24) | The circuit accelerates; friends and rival advance toward recruitment, live-rift licenses, and better-funded teams on paths Kiva cannot copy | Two-door transitions and blended ashcraft are attempted and fail; teams invest earned ash in training and gear; professional footage reveals the field ceiling | A developed learning fight against an adaptive rival is followed by a live Flask response where a new spawn expression threatens the arena grid and cannot be solved like a duel | First deliberate two-door sequence works briefly; the disk warms after; the team recovers a meaningful yield; another Unroofed Sky fragment appears but supplies no tactic |
| **4 — What the Record Lost** (Ch. 25–32) | Public results and unusual spawn yields make the anomaly searchable; founder histories, ash patents, and old fever accounts disagree | Noa, Orla, and Hollis pursue different evidence. Supporting cast makes independent placement and recruitment choices while the students qualify for deeper live work | A supervised professional shadow shift becomes a real multi-type Riftspawn call. Competition habits fail until declared roles and reasons return; clean recovery after the kill matters to the threatened district | Kiva sees professional rift fighting without spectacle's safety net, learns the disk was released to a bearer, and finds a pre-Kiva Unroofed Sky fragment |
| **5 — The Regional Measure** (Ch. 33–40) | Colleges, utilities, industry houses, school orders, and professional companies want ownership, exclusion, proof, and the season's unusually rich ash yield | Friendship fractures/reforms around risk, credit, sponsorship, kill responsibility, and ash shares; Kiva trains one controlled three-door transition with abort conditions | A long bracket alternates human combat, team spawn response, rescue, and ashcraft; filter instability introduces a stronger variety no event designer ordered, forcing rival teams to cooperate | The three-door configuration is publicly real but not mastered. Kiva permanently loses fine sensation in two fingers; no ranking or recovered ash erases it |
| **6 — Why It Is Crooked** (Ch. 41–48) | A consequential rift emergency hits the collegiate district and ash grid; students and professionals must cooperate | Earlier kill drills, recovery work, ash shutdown practice, and each supporting character's independent skill become the response plan | A higher-yield regional spawn expression emerges from the failing induction/rift boundary. Kiva sequences all three doors to stabilize the boundary while professionals and peers own the developed kill, rescue, clean-ash capture, and grid tasks | Emergency answered; regional season and ash ledger close; disk recognition, a bounded pro offer, and measured proof that human potential and spawn strength are rising together open the national Book Two |

## 5. Progression design

### First third

Teach the three doors, rank/skill distinction, soul/ash limits, ash induction,
magic/ash blending, collegiate rules, professional recruitment, classification, and
institutional stakes aggressively. Make the earned-ash loop concrete: kill, contain,
recover, grade, divide, invest. Let failed attempts and demonstrations carry the
exposition. Kiva is not secretly powerful; breadth creates control problems, fatigue,
poor efficiency, and team risk before it becomes an advantage. The first supervised
spawn kills should occupy developed scenes across several chapters, including practice,
fear, tactics, dissolution, and recovery—not a montage or a single summary paragraph.

### Middle third

Reduce vocabulary repetition and let relationships, competition, and tactics carry
the system. Two-door use appears only after single-door discipline is visible.

### Final third

Surface rules at true turning points. The first intentional three-door sequence is
short, costly, and planted. It proves configuration, not mastery or Glory.

## 6. Plant/payoff ledger

| Plant | Payoff |
|---|---|
| Three doors open; classification cannot settle | Public controlled sequence in Movement 6 |
| Crooked post retained without a clear reason | Founder record explains why; protagonist applies the principle rather than worshiping the object |
| Disk warms only after Kiva's first correct deliberate sequence | Orla and Noa recognize the appointed bearer line, not a cosmic origin |
| Rival's strong single-door and ashcraft model | Rival adapts and owns an essential climax task Kiva cannot replace |
| Friend's envy or outside goal | Friendship survives only after protagonist stops treating support as automatic |
| Home Line field guide doubts school metrics | Real emergency proves both metrics and judgment are needed |
| Conflicting copies of the reasons ledger | Missing reason exposes institutional drift and guides a repair |
| Filter measurement anomaly | Final emergency produces credible evidence of acceleration, not a full realm break |
| Ash induction powers Mara's shop, the colleges, and the arena | Grid failure in Movement 6 has understood mechanisms, shutdowns, and civic stakes |
| Professional standings count rescue, closure, infrastructure, and clean ash | Kiva's first pro offer recognizes judgment rather than kill count |
| Team Seven's first small Handful yield share | The team chooses what to spend, save, and return; later rich yields strain loyalty and recruitment |
| Students and spawn both appear stronger than recent records predict | The filter admits increasing Glory pressure, raising human potential and spawn variety together |
| Kiva sees converging Glory figures and an absent sky in the 2676 fever dream | Historical fragment in Movement 4 confirms shared imagery; Books Two and Three widen the convergence |
| Prologue shows radiation rotations, nuclear-ash synthesis, compact cores, and flight | Every later grid, arena, rescue, and industrial conflict rests on understood historical mechanisms |

## 7. Movement One packet rule

The names and six opening locks in `STATE_LEDGER.md` are resolved. Movement One now
receives entry state, pressure, boundaries, output paths, and the whole eight-chapter
run—never forty-eight miniature chapter contracts.
